\(d=\frac37\), the implementation listed under Code availability, in exact
rational arithmetic on one processor core, computes all principal parts of
\(H_1,\dots,H_{12}\) in about fifteen seconds; the Shapovalov matrix at
level~\(12\) has \(p(12)=77\) rows.
\end{remark*}

\begin{remark*}[What the chain values mean]
By Corollary~\ref{cor:chain-values}, $c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,0}(b_\star)$ is the
endpoint coefficient in the recursion~\eqref{eq:fixed-charge} at the level
$\mu-a$, and it is determined, level by level, by the inverse Shapovalov forms
and the torus forms at $c_\star$ (Definitions~\ref{def:matrices}
and~\ref{def:eta-reduced}), after the contributions of the weights strictly
between $a$ and $\mu$ are subtracted; no deformation of the charge enters.
One can ask whether these values also have a direct meaning in the Verma
module, as the top coefficient has.  At an interior minimal-model weight
$\lambda$, the value $c^{(L)}_{\lambda\to\mu,0}(b_\star)$ from $\lambda$ itself
to a node $\mu$ of layer $L$ is $\mathcal N/\gamma$, a torus norm divided by a
Jantzen norm of one singular vector (Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}), and
Section~\ref{sec:diagonal} gives in closed form all values with $k+j$ equal to
the layer distance.  The corollary does not identify the other values with
matrix elements of, or pairings on, a single singular vector, and they need not
be polynomial multiples of the top coefficient.  In the example after
Definition~\ref{def:chains}
($c=0$, $\lambda=0$, $\mu=5$) both chain products have degree at most $10$ in
$d$, and $c^{(2)}_{0\to5,0}(b_\star)={\mathcal R}_{1,2}{\mathcal R}_{3,1}/7$ has
degree exactly $10$, since each $\mathcal N_{u,v}$ has $2uv$ factors linear in
$d$; so a polynomial quotient $c^{(1)}_{0\to5,0}(b_\star)/c^{(2)}_{0\to5,0}(b_\star)$
would be constant, but it equals $\frac{83478061}{11476080}$ at $d=\frac37$ and
$\frac{11152201}{1263780}$ at $d=\frac15$.
\end{remark*}

\section{Jantzen layers at minimal-model charges and the top pole}\label{sec:mm-layers}

Let \(p,p'\geq2\) be
coprime, let \(b_\star^2=-p'/p\), and let \(\lambda=h_{r,s}(b_\star)\) with
\(0<r<p\) and \(0<s<p'\).  The labels \((r,s)\) and \((p-r,p'-s)\) give the
same weight (Proposition~\ref{prop:minimal-collisions}); we choose the one with
\(n_1:=p'r+ps<pp'\), and put \(n_0:=p'r-ps\).  Define the layer levels
\begin{gather*}
 \ell^\pm_{2j}=j(jpp'\pm n_0)\quad(j\geq1),\\
 \ell^+_{2j+1}=(r+jp)(s+jp'),\qquad
 \ell^-_{2j+1}=((j+1)p-r)((j+1)p'-s)\quad(j\geq0),
\end{gather*}
put \(\ell_k:=\min(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)\), and define the \emph{Jantzen depth}
at level \(N\) as
\(j_N(\lambda):=\max(\{0\}\cup\{k\geq1:\ell_k\leq N\})\), the number of layers
that have reached level \(N\).  Put \(\ell^\pm_0=0\).  For \(j\geq0\),
expanding the layer levels gives
\[
 \begin{array}{lll}
  & \text{upper sign} & \text{lower sign}\\[2pt]
  \ell^+_{2j+1}-\ell^\pm_{2j} & s(r+2jp) & r(s+2jp')\\
  \ell^-_{2j+1}-\ell^\pm_{2j} & (p-r)((2j+1)p'-s) & (p'-s)((2j+1)p-r)\\
  \ell^\pm_{2j+2}-\ell^+_{2j+1} & (p'-s)((2j+1)p+r) & (p-r)((2j+1)p'+s)\\
  \ell^\pm_{2j+2}-\ell^-_{2j+1} & r(2(j+1)p'-s) & s(2(j+1)p-r)
 \end{array}
\]
and all these differences are positive, so \(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<\ell_{k+1}\)
for every \(k\geq0\).  Moreover \(\ell^+_{2j}-\ell^-_{2j}=2jn_0\neq0\) for
\(j\geq1\) (since \(p\nmid r\)), and
\(\ell^+_{2j+1}-\ell^-_{2j+1}=(2j+1)(n_1-pp')\neq0\) for \(j\geq0\), so
\(\ell^+_k\neq\ell^-_k\) for \(k\geq1\).  Figure~\ref{fig:braided} shows the embedding
diagram that these levels describe.

For an integer \(x\) put \(d_x=(x^2-(p'-p)^2)/(4pp')\), so that
\(h_{u,v}(b_\star)=d_{p'u-pv}\); we call \(|x|\) the \emph{Coulomb momentum} of
the weight \(d_x\).  The weight \(\lambda\) has momentum \(|n_0|\), and
\(\lambda+\ell^\pm_k=d_{m^\pm_k}\) with the positive integers
\[
 \begin{aligned}
  m^+_{2j+1}&=2jpp'+n_1,& m^-_{2j+1}&=2(j+1)pp'-n_1&&(j\geq0),\\
  m^+_{2j}&=2jpp'+n_0,& m^-_{2j}&=2jpp'-n_0&&(j\geq1),
 \end{aligned}
\]
which satisfy \(m^+_k+m^-_k=2kpp'\).

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.15cm,y=0.95cm,>=stealth,
  every node/.style={font=\small}]
 \node (l0) at (0,0) {\(\lambda\)};
 \node (m1) at (-1.6,-1.3) {\(\lambda+\ell^-_1\)};
 \node (p1) at (1.6,-1.3) {\(\lambda+\ell^+_1\)};
 \node (m2) at (-1.6,-2.6) {\(\lambda+\ell^-_2\)};
 \node (p2) at (1.6,-2.6) {\(\lambda+\ell^+_2\)};
 \node (m3) at (-1.6,-3.9) {\(\lambda+\ell^-_3\)};
 \node (p3) at (1.6,-3.9) {\(\lambda+\ell^+_3\)};
 \node (d1) at (-1.6,-4.9) {\(\vdots\)};
 \node (d2) at (1.6,-4.9) {\(\vdots\)};
 \draw[->] (l0)--(m1); \draw[->] (l0)--(p1);
 \draw[->] (m1)--(m2); \draw[->] (m1)--(p2);
 \draw[->] (p1)--(m2); \draw[->] (p1)--(p2);
 \draw[->] (m2)--(m3); \draw[->] (m2)--(p3);
 \draw[->] (p2)--(m3); \draw[->] (p2)--(p3);
 \node[anchor=west] at (3.2,-1.3) {layer \(1\)};
 \node[anchor=west] at (3.2,-2.6) {layer \(2\)};
 \node[anchor=west] at (3.2,-3.9) {layer \(3\)};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The braided embedding diagram of \(M(c_\star,\lambda)\) at an
interior weight \(\lambda\).  A node \(\mu\) stands for the Verma submodule
\(M(c_\star,\mu)\), and an arrow \(\mu\to\nu\) for the embedding
\(M(c_\star,\nu)\subset M(c_\star,\mu)\).  Each node of layer \(k\) has arrows to
both nodes of layer \(k+1\).  The two submodules of layer \(i\) span the
Jantzen layer \(J^i(c_\star,\lambda)\) (Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith}), and
\(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<\ell_{k+1}\).}
\label{fig:braided}
\end{figure}

\begin{theorem}[Smith layers at minimal-model charges]
\label{thm:jantzen-smith}
In this setting, \(M(c_\star,\lambda)\) has, for every \(k\geq1\), singular
vectors \(w^\pm_k\) of levels \(\ell^\pm_k\), and with
\(W^\pm_k=U(\Vir_-)w^\pm_k\), one has
\(J^i(c_\star,\lambda)=W^+_i+W^-_i\) for \(i\geq1\).  Consequently, at every
level \(N\), the number of local Smith exponents of \(G_N(c_\star,\lambda+x)\)
that are at least \(i\) is \(\dim(W^+_i+W^-_i)_N\).  The inverse Shapovalov
matrix \(G_N(c_\star,\Delta)^{-1}\) has a pole of order exactly
\(j_N(\lambda)\) at \(\Delta=\lambda\), and \(F_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)\) and
\(H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)\) have poles of order at most \(j_N(\lambda)\)
there.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
In the notation of~\cite[\S5.1.4]{IoharaKoga2011}, \(c_\star=c_{p,p'}\), and
\(p'r+ps<pp'\) puts \((r,s)\) in the interior set, so \((c_\star,\lambda)\) is in
Class~\(R^+\), Case~\(1+\).  Since \(c_\star\neq1,25\),
\cite[Theorem~6.3(i), (6.3)]{IoharaKoga2011} identifies \(J^i(c_\star,\lambda)\)
with the sum of the two Verma submodules of highest weights \(\xi_{\pm i}\) in
the braided diagram of~\cite[Proposition~5.4 and Figure~5.4]{IoharaKoga2011}.
By \cite[(5.4) and (5.22)]{IoharaKoga2011}, \(\xi_0=\lambda\)
and, for \(k\geq1\), the weights \(\xi_{\pm k}\) have squared Coulomb-gas
momenta \((2jpp'\mp n_0)^2\) when \(k=2j\), and \((2(j+1)pp'-n_1)^2\) and
\((2jpp'+n_1)^2\) when \(k=2j+1\), that is, \((m^\mp_k)^2\).  So
\(\xi_{\pm k}=\lambda+\ell^\mp_k\); note that the sign of \(\xi_{\pm k}\) is
opposite to that of the level.

The same layers follow from \cite[Theorem~B(e), case \(\mathrm{III}_-\)]{Astashkevich1997},
which applies because the line \(c=c_\star\) is transverse to every Kac
curve through \((\lambda,c_\star)\) (as \(c'(b_\star)\neq0\)).

By Lemmas~\ref{lem:jantzen-smith} and~\ref{lem:smith}, the pole order of
\(G_N^{-1}\) is the largest \(i\geq1\) with \((W^+_i+W^-_i)_N\neq0\), or zero if
there is none, and this space is
nonzero exactly when \(\min(\ell^+_i,\ell^-_i)\leq N\). The trace \(F_N\) has at most
this order, and so does \(H_N=\sum_j\eta_jF_{N-j}\) since \(j_{N-j}\leq j_N\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Top Jantzen layer]\label{thm:top-layer}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith}, put
\(\mu^\pm_k=\lambda+\ell^\pm_k\),
\(\gamma^\pm_k=B^k(w^\pm_k,w^\pm_k)\), and
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k(d)=\langle w^\pm_k|V_d(1)|w^\pm_k\rangle\).
Let \(k\geq1\) and \(\ell_k\leq N<\ell_{k+1}\).  Then \(j_N(\lambda)=k\);
for each sign with \(\ell^\pm_k\leq N\), one has \(\gamma^\pm_k\neq0\),
\(\det G_M(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k)\neq0\) for \(M\leq N-\ell^\pm_k\),
\(\deg_d\mathcal N^\pm_k=2\ell^\pm_k\), and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:top-layer}
 \lim_{\Delta\to\lambda}(\Delta-\lambda)^kH_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)
 =\sum_{\pm:\ \ell^\pm_k\leq N}\frac{\mathcal N^\pm_k(d)}{\gamma^\pm_k}\,
 H_{N-\ell^\pm_k}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k,d).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<\ell_{k+1}\) (see the table before
Figure~\ref{fig:braided}), \(j_N(\lambda)=k\) and
\(J^{k+1}_N(c_\star,\lambda)=0\); and \(\ell^+_k\neq\ell^-_k\).

Fix \(N'\) with \(\ell_k\leq N'\leq N\).  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith}, \(k\) is the largest local Smith exponent
of \(G_{N'}(c_\star,\lambda+x)\), and
\(J^k_{N'}(c_\star,\lambda)=(W^+_k)_{N'}+(W^-_k)_{N'}\).  For each sign with
\(\ell^\pm_k\leq N'\), the vectors \(L_{-Z}w^\pm_k\), with \(Z\) a partition
of \(N'-\ell^\pm_k\), form a basis of \((W^\pm_k)_{N'}\), because the
embedding \(M(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k)\to M(c_\star,\lambda)\) that sends the
highest-weight vector to \(w^\pm_k\) is injective
\cite[the paragraph preceding Corollary~5.2]{IoharaKoga2011}.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:top-coefficient}(i) and singularity of \(w^\pm_k\),
\[
 B^k(L_{-Z}w^\pm_k,L_{-Z'}w^\pm_k)=B^k(w^\pm_k,L_ZL_{-Z'}w^\pm_k)
 =\gamma^\pm_k\,G_{N'-\ell^\pm_k}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k)_{Z,Z'} .
\]
If both signs occur, let \(w\) and \(w'\) be the singular vectors of the
lower and the higher level.  Then \(L_ZL_{-Z'}w\) is a combination of
vectors \(L_{-Y}w\) with \(|Y|\) equal to the level of \(w'\) minus the level
of \(w\), which is positive, and
\(B^k(w',L_{-Y}w)=B^k(L_Yw',w)=0\); so the two summands are
\(B^k\)-orthogonal.  As \(B^k\) is nondegenerate on \(J^k_{N'}\) by
Lemma~\ref{lem:top-coefficient}(ii), the sum is direct and
\(\gamma^\pm_kG_{N'-\ell^\pm_k}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k)\) is invertible.  Letting
\(N'\) range from \(\ell^\pm_k\) to \(N\) proves the nonvanishing
assertions.

Since \(k\) is the pole order of \(G_{N'}^{-1}\) at \(\lambda\)
(Lemma~\ref{lem:smith}), Lemma~\ref{lem:top-coefficient}(ii) in the basis
above and Lemma~\ref{lem:singular-factorization} give
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 [x^{-k}]F_{N'}(c_\star,\lambda+x,d)
 &=\sum_{\pm}\frac1{\gamma^\pm_k}\sum_{Z,Z'}
 \bigl(G_{N'-\ell^\pm_k}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k)^{-1}\bigr)_{Z,Z'}
 \langle w^\pm_k|L_ZV_d(1)L_{-Z'}|w^\pm_k\rangle\\
 &=\sum_{\pm}\frac{\mathcal N^\pm_k(d)}{\gamma^\pm_k}\,
 F_{N'-\ell^\pm_k}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k,d),
 \end{aligned}
\]
where the sums run over signs with \(\ell^\pm_k\leq N'\).  For
\(N'<\ell_k\), \(F_{N'}\) has a pole of order at most
\(j_{N'}(\lambda)<k\) at \(\lambda\).  Multiplying by the Euler
coefficients and summing as in Definition~\ref{def:eta-reduced} gives
\eqref{eq:top-layer}.  The degree of \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) is
Lemma~\ref{lem:singular-degree}.
\end{proof}

The torus norms \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) factor into the generic norms of
Definition~\ref{def:generic-residue}.  Normalize each \(w^\pm_k\) to
\(L_{-1}^{\ell^\pm_k}\)-coefficient \(1\).  By
Lemmas~\ref{lem:regular-everywhere} and~\ref{lem:singular-factorization} and
\cite[Proposition~5.1]{IoharaKoga2011}, at \(b=b_\star\),
\[
 \mathcal N^+_{2j+1}={\mathcal N}_{r+jp,\,s+jp'},\qquad
 \mathcal N^-_{2j+1}={\mathcal N}_{(j+1)p-r,\,(j+1)p'-s},
\]
and for \(k=2j\),
\begin{gather*}
 \mathcal N^+_{2j}={\mathcal N}_{r+(j-1)p,\,s+(j-1)p'}\,{\mathcal N}_{(2j-1)p+r,\,p'-s},\\
 \mathcal N^-_{2j}={\mathcal N}_{r+(j-1)p,\,s+(j-1)p'}\,{\mathcal N}_{p-r,\,(2j-1)p'+s},
\end{gather*}
where, by Definition~\ref{def:generic-residue},
\[
 {\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d)=\prod_{a',l}(d-d_{p'a'-pl})(d-d_{p'a'+pl})
 \qquad(\text{odd }1\leq a'<2R,\ \text{odd }1\leq l<2S).
\]

\subsection{Coefficients on the diagonal}\label{sec:diagonal}

Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer} identifies the values
$c^{(L)}_{\lambda\to\mu,0}(b_\star)$ for the nodes $\mu$ of layer $L$.  This
subsection computes, for any two nodes $a,\mu$ of the diagram, all values
$c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)$ with $k+j$ equal to the layer distance
(Theorem~\ref{thm:diagonal}); at $a=\lambda$ they are the top coefficient times
a power of a momentum ratio times a universal rational number
(Corollary~\ref{cor:diagonal-lambda}).  Beyond the diagonal the values vanish:
for nodes $a,\mu$ at layer distance $D$, each chain term of
$c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}$ is of order $t^{k+j-D}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i)
below), so $c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)=0$ for $k+j>D$.  On the diagonal the
values come from the leading coefficients in $t$ of the residues $R_g$ and of
the shifts $\delta_g$ alone; below it, for $k+j<D$, they can also depend on
higher Taylor coefficients.  In~\eqref{eq:fixed-charge}, for the coefficient of
$(\Delta-\lambda)^{-k}$, the diagonal terms are those with the highest
derivative order allowed by the layer bound, $j=L_\mu-k$, at the nodes $\mu$
with $L_\mu\geq k$; their coefficients can still vanish (for instance
$f_1(0,3)=0$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:diagonal}).
Corollary~\ref{cor:chain-values} determines all values from data at $c_\star$;
on the diagonal this subsection gives them in closed form.

Keep the setting of this section and put $N_0=2pp'$.  The \emph{nodes} are
$\lambda$, of layer $0$, and the weights $\mu^\pm_k=\lambda+\ell^\pm_k$ of
Figure~\ref{fig:braided}, of layer $k\geq1$.  Write $L_\nu$ for the layer of a
node $\nu$ and $D(a,\mu)=L_\mu-L_a$ for the \emph{layer distance} when
$L_a<L_\mu$.  Chain functions from a node $a$ are those of
Definition~\ref{def:chains} with $a$ in place of $\lambda$.  The computations
of this subsection use the following data.
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item The \emph{signed momentum} $\tilde m_\nu$ of a node $\nu$:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \tilde m_\lambda=n_0,\qquad \tilde m(\mu^\pm_{2j})=n_0\pm jN_0,\\
 \tilde m(\mu^+_{2j+1})=n_1+jN_0,\qquad \tilde m(\mu^-_{2j+1})=n_1-(j+1)N_0,
 \end{gathered}
\]
  so that $|\tilde m(\mu^\pm_k)|=m^\pm_k$ and $\nu=d_{\tilde m_\nu}$.
\item The \emph{position} of a node: $\pi_\lambda=0$ and $\pi(\mu^\pm_k)=\pm k/2$.
\item The \emph{class} of a node, the parity of its layer, and the
  \emph{class centres} $\alpha=n_0/N_0$ (even layers) and
  $\beta=(n_1-pp')/N_0$ (odd layers).  Then
  $\tilde m_\nu=N_0(\alpha+\pi_\nu)$ for $L_\nu$ even and
  $\tilde m_\nu=N_0(\beta+\pi_\nu)$ for $L_\nu$ odd.
\item For a positive label $g=(u,v)$, the integers $x_g=p'u-pv$ and
  $y_g=p'u+pv$, so that $h_g(b_\star)=d_{x_g}$ and $h_g(b_\star)+\ell_g=d_{y_g}$;
  we say that $g$ \emph{leads to} $d_{y_g}$.
\end{itemize}
The positions are the same in every minimal model, and $\tilde m_\nu/N_0$ is
the position of $\nu$ plus a class centre, which depends on the model.  The
results below have the matching form: a top coefficient times a power of a
ratio of signed momenta times a rational number that depends only on
positions.  For nodes $x,y$ of the same
class, $\tilde m_x-\tilde m_y=N_0(\pi_x-\pi_y)$.  Since $|n_0|<n_1<pp'$, we have
$|\alpha|,|\beta|<\frac12$, so $|\tilde m_\nu|=N_0\bigl(|\pi_\nu|+\operatorname{sgn}(\pi_\nu)\mathfrak c_\nu\bigr)$
for $\nu\neq\lambda$, where $\mathfrak c_\nu\in\{\alpha,\beta\}$ is the centre of the
class of $\nu$.

\begin{lemma}[Labels between nodes]\label{lem:node-labels}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] For every positive label $g$,
$h'_g(b_\star)=\frac{b_\star}{2p'^2}(p'^2-p^2-x_gy_g)$, where $'$ is $d/db$.
\item[(ii)] Let $\nu,\nu'$ be nodes with $L_{\nu'}-L_\nu$ odd and
positive.  There is exactly one positive label of weight $\nu$ leading to
$\nu'$, namely
$g=\bigl(\epsilon(\tilde m_{\nu'}+\tilde m_\nu)/(2p'),\ \epsilon(\tilde m_{\nu'}-\tilde m_\nu)/(2p)\bigr)$
with $\epsilon=\operatorname{sgn}\tilde m_{\nu'}$, and $x_gy_g=\tilde m_\nu \tilde m_{\nu'}$.
\item[(iii)] Every positive label whose weight is a node leads to a node of
larger layer and of the other class.  So the chains from a node $a$ to a node
$\mu$ are the sequences of nodes $a=x_0,x_1,\dots,x_{n+1}=\mu$ in which every
step $L_{x_{i+1}}-L_{x_i}$ is odd and positive.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) From $h_{u,v}(b)=\frac14((b+b^{-1})^2-(ub+vb^{-1})^2)$,
$h'_{u,v}(b)=\frac12((1-u^2)b-(1-v^2)b^{-3})$, and
$b_\star^{-3}=b_\star p^2/p'^2$; also $p'^2u^2-p^2v^2=x_gy_g$.
(ii) $\tilde m_{\nu'}\pm \tilde m_\nu$ is congruent modulo $N_0$ to $\pm(n_1\pm n_0)$, and
$n_0+n_1=2p'r$, $n_1-n_0=2ps$; so $\tilde m_{\nu'}+\tilde m_\nu\equiv0\pmod{2p'}$ and
$\tilde m_{\nu'}-\tilde m_\nu\equiv0\pmod{2p}$, and both entries of $g$ are positive integers
because $|\tilde m_{\nu'}|>|\tilde m_\nu|$.  Then $x_g=\epsilon \tilde m_\nu$ and $y_g=|\tilde m_{\nu'}|$,
so $g$ has weight $\nu$ and leads to $\nu'$.  A label of weight $\nu$ leading
to $\nu'$ has $y_g=|\tilde m_{\nu'}|$ and $x_g=\pm \tilde m_\nu$, and $x_g=-\epsilon \tilde m_\nu$
would need $\tilde m_{\nu'}-\tilde m_\nu\equiv0\pmod{2p'}$, that is $p'\mid s$.
(iii) Since $y_g-x_g=2pv$ and $y_g+x_g=2p'u$, $y_g\equiv x_g\pmod{2p}$ and
$y_g\equiv-x_g\pmod{2p'}$; with $n_1\equiv n_0\pmod{2p}$ and
$n_1\equiv-n_0\pmod{2p'}$, a weight of momentum $\equiv\pm n_0$ leads to a
momentum $\equiv\pm n_1\pmod{N_0}$, and conversely.  Every integer
$\equiv\pm n_0$ or $\pm n_1\pmod{N_0}$ is $\pm \tilde m_\nu$ for a node $\nu$, and
$|\tilde m_\nu|$ increases with $L_\nu$ by $\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<\ell_{k+1}$, since
$d_x$ increases with $|x|$; and $y_g>|x_g|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pole orders]\label{lem:node-poles}
For the label $g$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:node-labels}(ii),
$R_g=\rho_g\,t^{1-D(\nu,\nu')}+O(t^{2-D(\nu,\nu')})$ with $\rho_g\neq0$ in $K_\star$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The factor $ab+eb^{-1}$ of ${\mathcal A}_{u,v}$ vanishes at $b_\star$ exactly
for $(a,e)=i(p,p')$, $i\neq0$, and then simply, its $b$-derivative being $2ip$.
With $(u,v)=g$, the conditions $ip\leq u$, $ip'\leq v$ for $i\geq1$ mean
$iN_0\leq|\tilde m_{\nu'}|-|\tilde m_\nu|$, and the conditions $-ip\geq1-u$, $-ip'\geq1-v$
mean $iN_0<|\tilde m_{\nu'}|-|\tilde m_\nu|$.  Now, with $D=D(\nu,\nu')$, $|\tilde m_{\nu'}|-|\tilde m_\nu|=\frac D2N_0+e$,
where $e$ is one of $\pm(n_1-pp')\pm n_0$ or, for $\nu=\lambda$,
$\pm(n_1-pp')-|n_0|$; from $n_1+n_0-pp'=2p'r-pp'$, $n_1-n_0-pp'=2ps-pp'$ and
$|n_0|<n_1<pp'$ one gets $|e|<pp'=N_0/2$.  So both counts equal $(D-1)/2$,
and ${\mathcal A}_g$ has a pole of order exactly $D-1$.  The factors of
${\mathcal N}_g(b_\star,d)$ are linear in $d$ with coefficient $-1$
(Definition~\ref{def:generic-residue}), so ${\mathcal N}_g(b_\star,d)\neq0$.
\end{proof}

For a chain $\gamma=(x_0,\dots,x_{n+1})$ from $a$ to $\mu$, with $g_i$ the label
from $x_i$ to $x_{i+1}$, put $\varsigma=b_\star/(2p'^2)$, $K=p'^2-p^2$ and
\[
 h_i=h'_{g_i}(b_\star)=\varsigma(K-\tilde m_{x_i}\tilde m_{x_{i+1}}),\qquad
 \rho_\gamma=\frac{\prod_{i=0}^n\rho_{g_i}}{\prod_{i=1}^n(h_{i-1}-h_i)}
\]
(Lemma~\ref{lem:node-labels}(i),(ii)); $\rho_\gamma$ is the leading coefficient
of $P^{(1)}_\gamma$ in $t$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i)), and
$\rho_\gamma=\rho_{g_0}$ for a one-label chain.  Here $h_{i-1}-h_i=\varsigma \tilde m_{x_i}(\tilde m_{x_{i+1}}-\tilde m_{x_{i-1}})\neq0$, since
$x_{i-1},x_{i+1}$ are of the same class and of different layers.  Put
$\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)=c^{(D)}_{a\to\mu,0}(b_\star)$ with $D=D(a,\mu)$,
and let $\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$ be the set of nodes $\theta$ with $L_\theta-L_a$
odd and either $L_\theta<L_\mu$ or $\theta=\mu$, the possible first steps.

\begin{lemma}[Leading terms and first steps]\label{lem:node-leading}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]
$P^{(k)}_\gamma\delta_{g_n}^j/j!=\rho_\gamma h_0^{k-1}h_n^j\,t^{k+j-D}/j!
+O(t^{k+j-D+1})$.  Hence $\sum_\gamma \rho_\gamma h_0^{k-1}h_n^j=0$ for
$k\geq1$, $j\geq0$, $k+j<D$, and
$c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)=\frac1{j!}\sum_\gamma \rho_\gamma h_0^{k-1}h_n^j$ for
$k+j=D$, the sums over the chains from $a$ to $\mu$.
\item[(ii)] $\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$ has $D$ elements, the numbers
$h_{a\theta}=\varsigma(K-\tilde m_a\tilde m_\theta)$ ($\theta\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$) are distinct,
and
\[
 F(\theta):=\sum_{\gamma:\,x_1=\theta}\rho_\gamma
 =\frac{\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)}
 {\prod_{\theta'\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)\setminus\{\theta\}}(h_{a\theta}-h_{a\theta'})}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) $\delta_g=h'_g(b_\star)t+O(t^2)$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:node-poles} the residues
give $t^{-\sum_i(D_i-1)}=t^{n+1-D}$ with $D_i=D(x_i,x_{i+1})$, and
the links give $t^{-n}$.  The vanishing is the regularity of
$c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:chain-regular}).  (ii) The odd layers
between $a$ and $\mu$ give $2\lfloor D/2\rfloor$ nodes, and $\mu$ is added when
$D$ is odd; the $\tilde m_\theta$ are distinct and $\tilde m_a\neq0$.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:node-labels}(iii) every chain has $x_1\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$,
and $h_0=h_{ax_1}$.  Part (i) with $(k,j)=(m+1,0)$ gives
$\sum_\theta F(\theta)h_{a\theta}^m=0$ for $m\leq D-2$ and
$=\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)$ for $m=D-1$; this Vandermonde system has the
stated solution, by Lagrange interpolation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Ratios of top coefficients]\label{prop:top-ratios}
Every $\operatorname{Top}(x\to y)$ is nonzero in $K_\star$.  For
$\theta\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$, $\theta\neq\mu$,
\[
 \operatorname{Top}(a\to\theta)\operatorname{Top}(\theta\to\mu)
 =\kappa(a,\theta,\mu)\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu),\qquad
 \kappa(a,\theta,\mu)=\Bigl(\frac{\tilde m_\theta}{\tilde m_a}\Bigr)^{D(\theta,\mu)}
 \hat\kappa(a,\theta,\mu),
\]
with
$\hat\kappa(a,\theta,\mu)=\prod_{x\in\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)}(\pi_x-\pi_a)\big/
\prod_{x\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)\setminus\mathrm{FS}(a\to\theta)}(\pi_x-\pi_\theta)$.
For a node $\nu$ with $L_\nu-L_a$ even and $L_a<L_\nu<L_\mu$, the same identity
holds with $\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)=(\tilde m_\nu/\tilde m_a)^{D(\nu,\mu)}\hat\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)$ and
a rational number $\hat\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)$ that depends only on the positions of
the nodes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A chain with $x_1=\theta\neq\mu$ is the label from $a$ to $\theta$ followed by
a chain $\gamma'$ from $\theta$ to $\mu$, and
$\rho_\gamma=\rho_{a\theta}\rho_{\gamma'}/(h_{a\theta}-h_{\theta x_2})$, where
$h_{a\theta}-h_{\theta\theta'}=\varsigma \tilde m_\theta(\tilde m_{\theta'}-\tilde m_a)\neq0$.  Grouping
by $x_2$, Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(ii) from the base $\theta$ and partial
fractions give
\[
 F(\theta)=\rho_{a\theta}\sum_{\theta'\in\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)}
 \frac{\operatorname{Top}(\theta\to\mu)}{(h_{a\theta}-h_{\theta\theta'})
 \prod_{\theta''\neq\theta'}(h_{\theta\theta'}-h_{\theta\theta''})}
 =\frac{\rho_{a\theta}\operatorname{Top}(\theta\to\mu)}
 {\prod_{\theta'\in\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)}(h_{a\theta}-h_{\theta\theta'})}.
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(ii) for the end $\theta$ and the one-label chain gives
$\rho_{a\theta}=\operatorname{Top}(a\to\theta)/\prod_{\tilde\theta}(h_{a\theta}-h_{a\tilde\theta})$
over $\tilde\theta\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\theta)\setminus\{\theta\}$; so
$\operatorname{Top}(x\to y)\neq0$ when $D(x,y)$ is odd.  Comparing with
Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(ii) for the end $\mu$, and using
$\mathrm{FS}(a\to\theta)\subset\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)$,
$h_{a\theta}-h_{a\tilde\theta}=\varsigma \tilde m_a(\tilde m_{\tilde\theta}-\tilde m_\theta)$ and
$|\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)|=|\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)\setminus\mathrm{FS}(a\to\theta)|=D(\theta,\mu)$,
gives the identity; the differences are of momenta of the same class, so the
factors $N_0$ cancel.  For $D(x,y)$ even take $\theta$ of layer $L_x+1$: then
$D(x,\theta)$ and $D(\theta,y)$ are odd, and the identity, whose left side is
nonzero, gives $\operatorname{Top}(x\to y)\neq0$.  For $\nu$ at even distance
from $a$ take $\theta$ of layer $L_a+1$; then $\nu\in\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)$,
$\theta\in\mathrm{FS}(a\to\nu)$, the three odd identities give
$\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)=\kappa(a,\theta,\mu)\kappa(\theta,\nu,\mu)/\kappa(a,\theta,\nu)$,
and $D(\theta,\mu)-D(\theta,\nu)=D(\nu,\mu)$ makes the powers of the signed momenta combine to
$(\tilde m_\nu/\tilde m_a)^{D(\nu,\mu)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Diagonal values]\label{lem:diagonal-values}
Let $D=D(a,\mu)$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] For $k+j=D$ and $j\geq1$,
$j\,c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)=c^{(k+1)}_{a\to\mu,j-1}(b_\star)
-\sum_{L_\nu=L_a+k}\operatorname{Top}(a\to\nu)\,c^{(1)}_{\nu\to\mu,j-1}(b_\star)$.
\item[(ii)] For $0\leq j\leq D-1$ the number
$f_j(a,\mu)=j!\,(\tilde m_a/\tilde m_\mu)^j\,c^{(D-j)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)/\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)$
is rational, depends only on $\pi_a$ and $\pi_\mu$ (not on $p,p',r,s$ or $d$),
and $f_0=1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) is~\eqref{eq:cut-value}.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i),
$c^{(k)}_{a\to\nu,0}$ is $O(t^{k-D(a,\nu)})$ and $c^{(1)}_{\nu\to\mu,j-1}$ is
$O(t^{j-D(\nu,\mu)})$; if $L_\nu<L_a+k$ the first vanishes at $b_\star$, and if
$L_\nu>L_a+k$ the second does.

(ii) We first write each $\rho_\gamma$ as $\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)$ times
a power of $\tilde m_a$ times a number that depends only on positions, and then
use the vanishing sums of Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i).

\emph{Step 1: the leading coefficients of the chains.}  Let
$\gamma=(x_0,\dots,x_{n+1})$ and $D_i=D(x_i,x_{i+1})$.  By the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios},
\[
 \rho_{g_i}=\frac{\operatorname{Top}(x_i\to x_{i+1})}
 {\prod_{\tilde\theta}\varsigma\tilde m_{x_i}(\tilde m_{\tilde\theta}-\tilde m_{x_{i+1}})}
 \qquad\bigl(\tilde\theta\in\mathrm{FS}(x_i\to x_{i+1})\setminus\{x_{i+1}\}\bigr),
\]
and by Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios}, applied inductively from the end,
\[
 \prod_{i=0}^n\operatorname{Top}(x_i\to x_{i+1})=\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)
 \prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\kappa(x_i,x_{i+1},\mu).
\]
Insert both into the definition of $\rho_\gamma$ and count the powers of the
signed momenta.  The power of $\tilde m_{x_i}$ is
$D(x_i,\mu)-D(x_{i+1},\mu)-(D_i-1)-1=0$ for $1\leq i\leq n-1$, and
$D_n-(D_n-1)-1=0$ for $x_n$, which has no outgoing $\kappa$; $\tilde m_a$ has
the power $-D(x_1,\mu)-(D_0-1)=1-D$, also for a one-label chain.  The remaining
factors are the ratios $\hat\kappa$ and differences of momenta of the same
class, $\sum_i(D_i-1)+n=D-1$ of them in the denominator, each $N_0$ times a
difference of positions.  So
\[
 \rho_\gamma=\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)\,(\varsigma N_0\tilde m_a)^{1-D}\,\hat\rho_\gamma
\]
with a rational number $\hat\rho_\gamma$ determined by the positions of the
nodes of $\gamma$.

\emph{Step 2: vanishing moments.}  Write $\tilde m_{x_1}=N_0(\mathfrak c_1+\pi_{x_1})$
and $\tilde m_{x_n}=N_0(\mathfrak c_2+\pi_{x_n})$, with $\mathfrak c_1,\mathfrak c_2\in\{\alpha,\beta\}$
fixed by the classes of $a$ and $\mu$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i),
\[
 \sum_\gamma\hat\rho_\gamma\,(K-\tilde m_a\tilde m_{x_1})^{k-1}
 (K-\tilde m_{x_n}\tilde m_\mu)^j=0\qquad(k+j<D).
\]
The two factors are affine in $\pi_{x_1}$ and $\pi_{x_n}$, with the nonzero
linear coefficients $-N_0\tilde m_a$ and $-N_0\tilde m_\mu$.  Taking
$(k,j)=(e+1,e')$ for $e+e'=0,1,\dots,D-2$ in turn gives
\[
 \sum_\gamma\hat\rho_\gamma\,\pi_{x_1}^{e}\pi_{x_n}^{e'}=0\qquad(e+e'\leq D-2).
\]

\emph{Step 3: the diagonal.}  For $k+j=D$, Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i)
gives $c^{(k)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)$ as
$\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)(N_0\tilde m_a)^{1-D}/j!$ times the first sum of Step~2,
now taken with $k+j=D$.
Expanding the two factors in the positions, Step~2 removes every term below
the top degree, and
\[
 f_j(a,\mu)=(-1)^{D-1}\sum_\gamma\hat\rho_\gamma\,\pi_{x_1}^{D-1-j}\pi_{x_n}^j .
\]
The chains from $a$ to $\mu$ are described by positions
(Lemma~\ref{lem:node-labels}(iii)), so this is a rational number that depends
only on $\pi_a$ and $\pi_\mu$.  Finally $f_0=1$ by the definition of
$\operatorname{Top}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Coefficients on the diagonal]\label{thm:diagonal}
For nodes $a,\mu$ with $D=D(a,\mu)\geq1$ and $0\leq j\leq D-1$,
\[
 c^{(D-j)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)=\frac{f_j(L_a,D)}{j!}
 \Bigl(\frac{\tilde m_\mu}{\tilde m_a}\Bigr)^{j}\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu),
\]
where, with $n=\lfloor(D-1)/2\rfloor$,
\[
 f_{2i}(l,D)=(-1)^i\,\frac{\binom ni\binom{l+n}i}{\binom{l+D-1}i},\qquad
 f_{2i+1}(l,D)=\begin{cases}f_{2i}(l,D)&D\text{ even},\\0&D\text{ odd},\end{cases}
\]
that is, $\sum_if_{2i}(l,D)x^i={}_2F_1(-n,-n-l;1-D-l;x)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{The recursion.}  Divide Lemma~\ref{lem:diagonal-values}(i) by
$\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)$ and use Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios}: with
$k=D-j$ and $\nu$ running over the two nodes of layer $L_a+k$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-rec}
 f_j(a,\mu)\tilde m_\mu=f_{j-1}(a,\mu)\tilde m_a
 -\sum_\nu\hat\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)f_{j-1}(\nu,\mu)\tilde m_\nu .
\end{equation}

\emph{Separating the classes.}  The rational numbers $f$ and $\hat\kappa$
depend only on positions and are the same in every minimal model
(Lemma~\ref{lem:diagonal-values}(ii), Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios}),
while each $\tilde m_x/N_0$ is the position of $x$ plus the centre of its
class.  So, after division by $N_0$, the difference of the two sides
of~\eqref{eq:diagonal-rec} is an affine function of the class centres
$(\alpha,\beta)$ whose coefficients do not depend on the model.  It vanishes
at the class centres of every model.  For the interior weights $h_{1,1}$ of
the models $(p,p')=(2,3),(2,5),(3,4)$ these are $(\frac1{12},-\frac1{12})$,
$(\frac3{20},-\frac3{20})$ and $(\frac1{24},-\frac5{24})$, three affinely
independent points, and an affine function of two variables that vanishes at
three affinely independent points is zero.  So we may compare
in~\eqref{eq:diagonal-rec} the coefficients of the class centre $\mathfrak c_A$ of the
class of $a$, of the centre $\mathfrak c_B$ of the other class, and the constant terms.

\emph{Induction.}  We argue by induction on $D$, for all bases at once; $D=1$
is $f_0=1$.  Write $l=L_a$.

\emph{$D$ odd, $j$ odd.}  Then $\mu$ is of class $B$ and the nodes $\nu$ ($k$
even) of class $A$; the $\mathfrak c_B$-coefficient of~\eqref{eq:diagonal-rec} is
$f_j(a,\mu)=0$.

\emph{$D$ even, $j$ odd.}  Now $\mu$ is of class $A$ and $\nu$ of class $B$;
the $\mathfrak c_A$-coefficient gives $f_j(a,\mu)=f_{j-1}(a,\mu)$, and then the constant
term gives
$f_j(a,\mu)(\pi_\mu-\pi_a)=-f_{j-1}(l+k,j)\sum_\theta\hat\kappa(a,\theta,\mu)\pi_\theta$,
where $f_{j-1}(\theta,\mu)=f_{j-1}(l+k,j)$ by induction, as $D(\theta,\mu)=j<D$.

\emph{$D$ odd, $j$ even, $j\geq2$.}  The $\mathfrak c_B$-coefficient gives
$f_j(a,\mu)=-f_{j-1}(l+k,j)\sum_\theta\hat\kappa(a,\theta,\mu)$.

These determine every $f_j(a,\mu)$ from $f_0=1$ and the values at smaller $D$
(for $D$ even and $j$ even, $f_j=f_{j+1}$).

\emph{The two sums.}  It remains to evaluate $\sum_\theta\hat\kappa$ and
$\sum_\theta\hat\kappa\,\pi_\theta$.  Let $\theta_\pm$ be the nodes of layer
$l+k$ ($k$ odd), at positions $\pm\upsilon$ with $\upsilon=\frac{l+k}2$, and note
$|\pi_a|=\frac l2$.  The nodes of $\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)$ of class $A$
other than $\mu$ come in pairs $\pm\frac m2$ over the layers $m=l+k+1+2e$
($e\geq0$), each pair contributing
$\pi_a^2-\frac{m^2}4=-(\frac{k+1}2+e)(l+\frac{k+1}2+e)$.  The set
$\mathrm{FS}(a\to\mu)\setminus\mathrm{FS}(a\to\theta_\epsilon)$ consists of
$\theta_{-\epsilon}$, the pairs over the layers $m'=l+k+2e'$ ($e'\geq1$) of
class $B$, contributing $-(\frac{m'^2}4-\upsilon^2)=-e'(l+k+e')$, and $\mu$ if
$D$ is odd.  Put
\[
 \Xi=\binom ni\frac{(l+n)!\,(l+2n-2i+1)!}{(l+n-i)!\,(l+2n-i+1)!}.
\]

For $D=2n+1$ and $j=2i$ ($k=2n-2i+1$, $\frac{k+1}2=n-i+1$) there are $i$ pairs
of class $A$ and $i-1$ of class $B$, and
\[
 \hat\kappa(a,\theta_\epsilon,\mu)=
 \frac{(-1)^i\frac{n!}{(n-i)!}\frac{(l+n)!}{(l+n-i)!}}
 {(-2\epsilon\upsilon)\,(-1)^{i-1}(i-1)!\,(l+k+1)_{i-1}\,(\pi_\mu-\epsilon\upsilon)} .
\]
Since $\sum_\epsilon\frac1{-2\epsilon\upsilon(\pi_\mu-\epsilon\upsilon)}=-\frac1{\pi_\mu^2-\upsilon^2}$
and $\pi_\mu^2-\upsilon^2=i(l+k+i)$, $\sum_\theta\hat\kappa=\Xi$.  With
$f_{2i-1}(l+k,2i)=(-1)^{i-1}\binom{l+k+i-1}{i-1}/\binom{l+k+2i-1}{i-1}$
(induction) this gives
\[
 f_{2i}(l,2n+1)=(-1)^i\Xi\,\frac{(l+2n-i)!\,(l+2n-i+1)!}{(l+2n-2i+1)!\,(l+2n)!}
 =(-1)^i\binom ni\binom{l+n}i\Big/\binom{l+2n}i .
\]

For $D=2n+2$ and $j=2i+1$ ($k=2n-2i+1$) there are $i$ pairs of class $A$, the
node $\mu$ in $\mathrm{FS}(\theta\to\mu)$, and $i$ pairs of class $B$:
\[
 \hat\kappa(a,\theta_\epsilon,\mu)=
 \frac{(-1)^i\frac{n!}{(n-i)!}\frac{(l+n)!}{(l+n-i)!}\,(\pi_\mu-\pi_a)}
 {(-2\epsilon\upsilon)\,(-1)^i\,i!\,(l+k+1)_i},
\]
so $\sum_\theta\hat\kappa\,\pi_\theta=-\Xi(\pi_\mu-\pi_a)$, and with
$f_{2i}(l+k,2i+1)=(-1)^i\binom{l+k+i}i/\binom{l+k+2i}i$ (induction),
$f_{2i+1}(l,2n+2)=f_{2i}(l+k,2i+1)\,\Xi=(-1)^i\binom ni\binom{l+n}i/\binom{l+2n+1}i$.
Here $(l+k+1)_i=(l+2n-i+1)!/(l+2n-2i+1)!$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Diagonal coefficients at $\lambda$]\label{cor:diagonal-lambda}
For $L\geq1$, $0\leq j\leq L-1$ and each sign,
\[
 c^{(L-j)}_{\lambda\to\mu^\pm_L,j}(b_\star)
 =\frac{f_j(0,L)}{j!}\Bigl(\pm\frac{m^\pm_L}{n_0}\Bigr)^{j}
 \frac{\mathcal N^\pm_L}{\gamma^\pm_L},\qquad
 f_{2i}(0,L)=(-1)^i\frac{\binom ni^2}{\binom{L-1}i},\quad
 n=\Bigl\lfloor\frac{L-1}2\Bigr\rfloor .
\]
In particular $c^{(1)}_{\lambda\to\mu^\pm_2,1}(b_\star)
=\pm(m^\pm_2/n_0)\,\mathcal N^\pm_2/\gamma^\pm_2$, and with $w^\pm_2$
normalized to $L_{-1}^{\ell^\pm_2}$-coefficient $1$,
\[
 \gamma^\pm_2=\mp\frac{n_1}{n_0}\,
 \frac1{{\mathcal A}_{r,s}(b_\star)\,{\mathcal A}_{p\pm r,\,p'\mp s}(b_\star)} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
$\tilde m(\mu^\pm_L)=\pm m^\pm_L$ and $\tilde m_\lambda=n_0$.  To identify
$\operatorname{Top}(\lambda\to\mu^\pm_L)$, take $\ell^\pm_L\leq N<\ell_{L+1}$
in~\eqref{eq:fixed-charge} with $k=L$.  The chain functions
$c^{(L)}_{\lambda\to\mu,j}$ with $L_\mu<L$, or with $L_\mu=L$ and $j\geq1$,
vanish at $b_\star$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(i)); no node of layer larger
than $L$ has level at most $N$; and $H_{N-\ell^\pm_L}(c_\star,\cdot)$ is regular
at $\mu^\pm_L$ because $N-\ell^\pm_L<\ell_{L+1}-\ell^\pm_L$.  So
$A^\star_{\lambda,L,N}=\sum c^{(L)}_{\lambda\to\mu^\pm_L,0}(b_\star)
H_{N-\ell^\pm_L}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_L,d)$ over the signs with $\ell^\pm_L\leq N$.
Compare with~\eqref{eq:top-layer} at $N=\min(\ell^+_L,\ell^-_L)$, where one sign
occurs and $H_0=1$, and then at $N=\max(\ell^+_L,\ell^-_L)$ after subtracting
the identified term: $\operatorname{Top}(\lambda\to\mu^\pm_L)=\mathcal
N^\pm_L/\gamma^\pm_L$.  For $L=2$, Lemma~\ref{lem:node-labels}(ii) gives the
labels $(r,s)$ from $\lambda$ to $\mu^+_1$ and $(p\pm r,p'\mp s)$ from $\mu^+_1$
to $\mu^\pm_2$, both with regular residues, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios} with $\theta=\mu^+_1$
($\hat\kappa=\mp1$, $\kappa=\mp n_1/n_0$) gives
$\operatorname{Top}(\lambda\to\mu^\pm_2)=\mp\frac{n_0}{n_1}R_{r,s}(b_\star)
R_{p\pm r,p'\mp s}(b_\star)$.  Since $R_g={\mathcal A}_g{\mathcal N}_g$ and
$\mathcal N^\pm_2={\mathcal N}_{r,s}{\mathcal N}_{p\pm r,p'\mp s}$ at $b_\star$
(the factorization after Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}), cancelling these nonzero
polynomials in $d$ gives $\gamma^\pm_2$.
\end{proof}

For $L=3,4,5$ the factors $f_j(0,L)/j!$ are $(1,0,-\frac14)$,
$(1,1,-\frac16,-\frac1{18})$ and $(1,0,-\frac12,0,\frac1{144})$.  At $c=0$,
$\lambda=0$ ($n_0=1$, $n_1=5$) the corollary gives
$\gamma^-_2=5/({\mathcal A}_{1,1}{\mathcal A}_{1,4}(b_\star))
=5/(\frac12\cdot(-\frac3{1120}))=-\frac{11200}3$, the value in the example
after Theorem~\ref{thm:general-confluence}.

\subsection{Boundary weights}\label{sec:boundary}

Theorems~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith} and~\ref{thm:top-layer} and
Section~\ref{sec:diagonal} treat the interior weights, whose embedding diagram
is braided.  At the boundary weights of Class~\(R^+\) the embedding diagram is
a single chain of submodules, one in each layer, and this subsection gives
there the top chain values, the chain values on the diagonal, and the exact
pole order for indeterminate \(d\) with its top coefficient
(Theorem~\ref{thm:boundary}).

Let $\lambda=d_{x_0}$, where $x_0$ is a positive multiple of exactly one of
$p,p'$ and $x_0<pp'$.  Put $\epsilon=1$ if $p\mid x_0$ and $\epsilon=-1$ if
$p'\mid x_0$, and for $k\geq0$
\[
 \pi_k=(-1)^k\lceil k/2\rceil,\qquad \tilde m_k=x_0+\pi_kN_0,\qquad m_k=|\tilde m_k|,\qquad
 \mu_k=d_{m_k},
\]
so $m_0=x_0$, $m_1=N_0-x_0$, $m_2=N_0+x_0,\dots$ increase with $k$ and are the
positive elements of $\pm x_0+N_0\mathbb Z$.  The $\mu_k$ are the nodes, $\mu_k$ of
layer $k$, and $\mu_0=\lambda$.  Every weight $d_x$ with exactly one of
$p\mid x$, $p'\mid x$ is a node $\mu_i$ for exactly one such $x_0$, the element
of $\pm x+N_0\mathbb Z$ of smallest absolute value (it is not $pp'$, which both
divide); for instance $d_{10}=\frac{33}8$ at $(p,p')=(2,3)$ is $\mu_1$ for
$x_0=2$.  These are the boundary weights of Class $R^+$ of
Section~\ref{sec:scope}.  For nodes $\mu_i,\mu_k$ with $i<k$ put
$D(\mu_i,\mu_k)=k-i$ and $\operatorname{Top}(\mu_i\to\mu_k)=
c^{(k-i)}_{\mu_i\to\mu_k,0}(b_\star)$.

\begin{lemma}[Labels at a boundary weight]\label{lem:boundary-labels}
For $0\leq i<k$ there is exactly one positive label $g$ of weight $\mu_i$
leading to $\mu_k$; it has $y_g=m_k$, $x_g=-\epsilon\operatorname{sgn}(\tilde m_k)\tilde m_i$ and
$x_gy_g=-\epsilon\tilde m_i\tilde m_k$, and $R_g=\rho_g\,t^{1-(k-i)}+O(t^{2-(k-i)})$ with
$\rho_g\neq0$ in $K_\star$.  Every positive label whose weight is a node $\mu_i$
leads to a node $\mu_k$ with $k>i$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $p\mid x_g$ then $y_g\equiv x_g\equiv-x_g\pmod{2p}$ and
$y_g\equiv-x_g\pmod{2p'}$, so $y_g\equiv-x_g\pmod{N_0}$; if $p'\mid x_g$ then
$y_g\equiv x_g\pmod{N_0}$.  A label of weight $\mu_i$ has $x_g=\pm \tilde m_i$, so
$y_g\equiv-\epsilon x_g$, and $y_g>|x_g|$ makes $y_g=m_k$ with $k>i$.  For given
$k$, $x_g\equiv-\epsilon\operatorname{sgn}(\tilde m_k)x_0\pmod{N_0}$ fixes the sign,
since $2x_0\not\equiv0\pmod{N_0}$.  Conversely, for these values
$y_g\pm x_g=\operatorname{sgn}(\tilde m_k)(\tilde m_k\mp\epsilon \tilde m_i)$, which is $\equiv0$ or
$\equiv2\operatorname{sgn}(\tilde m_k)x_0\pmod{N_0}$, and $2x_0$ is divisible by $2p$
if $\epsilon=1$ and by $2p'$ if $\epsilon=-1$; so $u,v$ are positive integers.
As in Lemma~\ref{lem:node-poles}, the pole order of ${\mathcal A}_g$ is
$\#\{i'\geq1:i'N_0\leq m_k-m_i\}+\#\{i'\geq1:i'N_0<m_k-m_i\}$.  For
$D=k-i$ even, $m_k-m_i=\frac D2N_0$; for $D$ odd,
$m_k-m_i=\frac{D-1}2N_0+(m_{i+1}-m_i)$ with
$m_{i+1}-m_i\in\{2x_0,N_0-2x_0\}$.  Both give $D-1$, and
${\mathcal N}_g(b_\star,d)\neq0$ as before.
\end{proof}

With this lemma in place of Lemmas~\ref{lem:node-labels}(ii),(iii)
and~\ref{lem:node-poles}, the proofs of Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading},
Proposition~\ref{prop:top-ratios} and Lemma~\ref{lem:diagonal-values} apply
with the signed momenta $\tilde m(\mu_k)=\tilde m_k$ and $h_{a\theta}=\varsigma(K+\epsilon\tilde m_a\tilde m_\theta)$; now
every node strictly between $a$ and $\mu$, and $\mu$ itself, is a first step,
one per layer, and the signs $\epsilon$ cancel in $f_j(a,\mu)$, which depends
only on the positions $\pi$ of the nodes.

\begin{theorem}[Boundary weights]\label{thm:boundary}
Let $a=\mu_i$, $\mu=\mu_k$, $D=k-i\geq1$, and let $g$ be the label from $\mu_i$
to $\mu_k$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu)=\rho_g\prod_{i<n<k}(h_{a\mu}-h_{a\mu_n})
=\rho_g(\epsilon\varsigma N_0\tilde m_i)^{D-1}\prod_{i<n<k}(\pi_k-\pi_n)$, which is
nonzero in $K_\star$.
\item[(ii)] For $0\leq j\leq D-1$,
\[
 c^{(D-j)}_{a\to\mu,j}(b_\star)=\frac1{j!}\Bigl(\frac{\tilde m_k}{\tilde m_i}\Bigr)^{j}
 \prod_{m=1}^j\frac{\pi_i-\pi_{k-m}}{\pi_k-\pi_{k-m}}\,\operatorname{Top}(a\to\mu).
\]
\item[(iii)] Put $\ell_m=\mu_{i+m}-\mu_i$ and let $\ell_D\leq N<\ell_{D+1}$.
Then $A^\star_{\mu_i,k',N}=0$ for $k'>D$, and $A^\star_{\mu_i,D,N}$ is
$\operatorname{Top}(\mu_i\to\mu_{i+D})$ times
$H_{N-\ell_D}(c_\star,\mu_{i+D},d)$.  This is nonzero in $K_\star$, so
$H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)$ has a pole of order exactly $D$ at $\Delta=\mu_i$.  At a
specialized $d$ the order is at most $D$, and exactly $D$ if and only if
$\operatorname{Top}(\mu_i\to\mu_{i+D})\,H_{N-\ell_D}(c_\star,\mu_{i+D},d)\neq0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) is Lemma~\ref{lem:node-leading}(ii) for the one-label chain, with
$h_{a\mu}-h_{a\mu_n}=\epsilon\varsigma\tilde m_i(\tilde m_k-\tilde m_n)$.  (ii) The analogue
of~\eqref{eq:diagonal-rec} has one node $\nu=\mu_{k-j}$ in the layer $L_a+D-j$:
$f_j(a,\mu)\tilde m_k=f_{j-1}(a,\mu)\tilde m_i-\hat\kappa(a,\nu,\mu)f_{j-1}(\nu,\mu)\tilde m_{k-j}$.
Here $\tilde m_n/N_0=\alpha_0+\pi_n$ for all $n$, with $\alpha_0=x_0/N_0$, and
$f$ and $\hat\kappa$ depend only on positions.  As in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:diagonal}, the difference of the two sides, divided by $N_0$,
is an affine function of $\alpha_0$ with coefficients that do not depend on the
model; it vanishes at the two boundary weights $x_0=2,4$ of $(p,p')=(2,3)$
($\alpha_0=\frac16,\frac13$), hence identically.  Its two coefficients give
$f_j=f_{j-1}-\hat\kappa f_{j-1}(\nu,\mu)$ and
$f_j\pi_k=f_{j-1}\pi_i-\hat\kappa f_{j-1}(\nu,\mu)\pi_{k-j}$.  Eliminating the
last term, $f_j(a,\mu)(\pi_k-\pi_{k-j})=f_{j-1}(a,\mu)(\pi_i-\pi_{k-j})$, and
induction from $f_0=1$ gives (ii).  (iii) Apply~\eqref{eq:fixed-charge} at the
weight $\mu_i$, which lies in $\operatorname{Kac}_N(b_\star)$ as the weight of the
label to $\mu_{i+1}$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-labels} the chains from
$\mu_i$ end at nodes $\mu_{i+m}$, and
$c^{(k')}_{\mu_i\to\mu_{i+m},j}(b_\star)=0$ when $k'+j>m$; only $m\leq D$ occurs,
and for $k'=D$ only $\mu_{i+D}$ with $j=0$ remains.  If $N=\ell_D$ this is the
first term of~\eqref{eq:fixed-charge}, and $H_0=1$.  Otherwise the zeros of the
Kac determinant at the level $M=N-\ell_D$ and the weight $\mu_{i+D}$ are labels
from $\mu_{i+D}$, of level at least $\ell_{D+1}-\ell_D>M$, so
$H_M(c_\star,\cdot)$ is regular at $\mu_{i+D}$ and~\eqref{eq:regvalue} gives the
value $H_M(c_\star,\mu_{i+D},d)$; as $\det G_M(c_\star,\mu_{i+D})\neq0$ and
$\mu_{i+D}$ has integer momentum, it is a nonzero polynomial in $d$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iii).  Since $H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)$ is a
polynomial in $d$ with coefficients rational in $\Delta$
(Section~\ref{sec:blocks}), each $A^\star_{\mu_i,k',N}$ is a polynomial in $d$, and
the coefficient of $(\Delta-\mu_i)^{-k'}$ in $H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d_0)$ is
$A^\star_{\mu_i,k',N}(d_0)$; this gives the assertion at a specialized $d$.
\end{proof}

At a specialized $d$ the top coefficient can vanish: the factors of
${\mathcal N}_g$ with $(k,l)=(1,1)$ are $-d$ and $1-d$.  The Jantzen filtration
at these weights is the classical one-chain filtration,\footnote{See
\cite[Theorem~6.3(ii)--(iv) and (6.4)]{IoharaKoga2011} and
\cite[Theorem~B]{Astashkevich1997}.} and the pole order $D$ of
$G_N(c_\star,\cdot)^{-1}$ at $\mu_i$ for $\ell_D\leq N<\ell_{D+1}$ follows from
it; contracting with the torus form can only lower this order.
Theorem~\ref{thm:boundary} shows that the block keeps the order $D$ over $K_\star$,
and gives its top coefficient: at the level $N=\ell_D$ it is
$\operatorname{Top}(\mu_i\to\mu_{i+D})$, the leading coefficient
$\rho_g=\operatorname{lead}({\mathcal A}_g){\mathcal N}_g(b_\star,d)$ of the single
residue from $\mu_i$ to $\mu_{i+D}$ times a product of slope differences, and at
larger $N$ it is this times $H_{N-\ell_D}(c_\star,\mu_{i+D},d)$.  For
example at $c=0$ the weight $\lambda=d_2=\frac18$ has nodes at the levels
$4,8,20,28,\dots$, and
$c^{(1)}_{\lambda\to\mu_2,1}(b_\star)=\frac{14}2\cdot\frac{2+10}{14+10}
\operatorname{Top}(\lambda\to\mu_2)=\frac72\operatorname{Top}(\lambda\to\mu_2)$.

\section{Sharpness of the Jantzen bound}\label{sec:sharpness}

By Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, the pole order of \(H_N\) at an interior
weight equals \(j_N(\lambda)\) as soon as the right side
of~\eqref{eq:top-layer} is a nonzero polynomial in \(d\).  On a level where
only one layer vector contributes, this follows from the nonvanishing of the
shifted block (Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}), and at
\(N=\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)\) from the orders of vanishing at \(d=0\) (proof
of Corollary~\ref{cor:top-layer-sharp}).  On a strict two-vector level,
\(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<N<\ell_{k+1}\), the right side is the sum of the two
products
\(\mathcal N^\pm_kH_{M^\pm}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k,d)/\gamma^\pm_k\) with
\(M^\pm=N-\ell^\pm_k\geq1\), and Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer} does not exclude
that they cancel.

To show that they do not, it suffices to find one value of \(d\) and one
Taylor coefficient there at which the sum is nonzero.  At \(d=0\) both
products vanish to order at least \(k+1\), and up to a nonzero factor the
coefficient of \(d^{k+1}\) is a sum or difference of two sums, each of either
sign, which we cannot show to be nonzero; so we work at other values of
\(d\).

Part~II proves the same sharpness for the sphere block by a shorter route
(Theorems~\ref{thm:sharp-B} and~\ref{thm:sharp-H}).  There the four external
weights can be chosen so that the three-point factor of the higher singular
vector vanishes, which removes one of the two terms.  For \(\mathcal B_N\) the
surviving shifted coefficient is then evaluated through a solution of a BPZ
equation; for \(H_N\) its leading coefficient in an external momentum is a
nonzero value of a Hermite polynomial.  The torus block has the single
external weight \(d\), and one value of \(d\) would have to do both things.
The values at which the shifted torus blocks are under control are the
degenerate weights \(h_{1,\pm3}(b_\star)\) below, after exchanging \(b\) and
\(b^{-1}\) (\(p\leftrightarrow p'\), \(r\leftrightarrow s\)) if needed so that
\(p'\) has an odd prime factor.  In that orientation the two norms
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) vanish at \(h_{1,3}(b_\star)\) to the same order \(k\)
unless \(s\in\{1,p'-1\}\).  When \(s\in\{1,p'-1\}\) (Case~1 of
Proposition~\ref{prop:two-term-criteria}) the orders differ, the term of
larger order drops out of the lowest Taylor coefficient at
\(h_{1,3}(b_\star)\), and the surviving shifted block is nonzero there; this
is the torus analogue of the sphere argument.  For the other \(s\) neither
term drops out, and the two terms have to be compared.

The proof below uses the external weights \(d_1=h_{1,3}(b_\star)\) and
\(d_2=h_{1,-3}(b_\star)\) instead, where the size of each shifted block at an
odd prime \(\varpi\) dividing \(p'\) is known.  At these weights the generic
recursion keeps only the labels \((u,1)\).  In this section and in
Appendix~\ref{app:h13} we write \(t=b^2\) (in Sections~\ref{sec:all-order}
and~\ref{sec:mm-layers}, \(t\) was \(b-b_\star\)).  After its denominators are
cleared, \(H_M\) becomes a polynomial in \(t\) and \(Y=\Delta t^2\) with
coefficients in \(\mathbb Z[\frac12]\) and constant term \(4^M\)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(i)).  At \(b=b_\star\) and
\(\Delta=\mu\) both \(t\) and \(Y\) are divisible by \(\varpi\), so this
polynomial takes a \(\varpi\)-adic unit value, and the \(\varpi\)-adic
valuation of \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d_\nu)\) is minus that of the cleared
denominator, given by~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation}.  When the last sum in~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation}
vanishes, this valuation is \(-v_\varpi(M!)-Mv_\varpi(p')\), which decreases strictly
with \(M\).  The torus norms are products of linear forms in \(d\)
(Definition~\ref{def:generic-residue}), so the valuations of their Taylor
coefficients at \(d_1\), and through Proposition~\ref{prop:layer-coefficients}
those of the Jantzen norms, can be counted factor by factor.

Proposition~\ref{prop:two-term-criteria} splits into three cases by \(s\).  In
Case~2, the main one, the count shows that
\([(d-d_1)^k]\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k\) has the same valuation for both
signs, and the last sum in~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation} vanishes.  Both products
vanish to order \(k\) at \(d_1\), and their Taylor coefficients of degree \(k\)
there then have valuations differing by
\(-v_\varpi(M^+!)+v_\varpi(M^-!)-(M^+-M^-)v_\varpi(p')\neq0\), and two rational numbers
of different valuation cannot cancel.  Case~1 is simpler: the two torus norms
vanish to different orders at \(d_1\).  In Case~3 the last sum
in~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation} can make the two valuations equal.  The proof then
compares the ratio of the two leading Taylor coefficients at \(d_1\) with the
ratio at \(d_2\), using Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iv) to control
how the ratio of shifted blocks changes with the level.

In the Lee--Yang model, \((p,p')=(2,5)\), at
\(\lambda=h_{1,2}(b_\star)=-\frac15\), the first strict two-vector level
falls under Case~2 and shows it in numbers.  The first
layer has singular vectors at levels \(2\) and \(3\), so
\(\mu^+_1=\frac95\) and \(\mu^-_1=\frac{14}5\), and the second layer starts at
level \(9\).  At \(N=4\) one has \(M^+=2\), \(M^-=1\), \(\varpi=5\) and
\(s=2\), and \(d_1=h_{1,3}(b_\star)=-\frac15\) is the weight of the Lee--Yang
field itself.  Both products vanish to first order at \(d_1\), and their first
Taylor coefficients there are
\begin{align*}
 \frac{[(d-d_1)]\mathcal N^+_1}{\gamma^+_1}\,H_2\bigl(c_\star,\tfrac95,d_1\bigr)
 &=\frac3{14}\cdot\frac{349}{425}=\frac{1047}{5950},\\
 \frac{[(d-d_1)]\mathcal N^-_1}{\gamma^-_1}\,H_1\bigl(c_\star,\tfrac{14}5,d_1\bigr)
 &=-\frac13\cdot\frac3{70}=-\frac1{70}.
\end{align*}
The norm ratios \(\frac3{14}\) and \(-\frac13\) are \(5\)-adic units.  The
shifted blocks have \(5\)-adic valuations \(-2\) and \(-1\),
as~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation} gives with \(a=1\), so the sum
\(\frac{481}{2975}\) is nonzero.  (Here \(\gamma^+_1=-\frac{84}{25}\),
\(\gamma^-_1=\frac{4536}{625}\) and \(H_1(c,\Delta,d)=d(d-1)/(2\Delta)\).)

\begin{proposition}[Layer coefficients at \(d=0\)]
\label{prop:layer-coefficients}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, let \(m^\pm_k\) be the
Coulomb momentum of \(\lambda+\ell^\pm_k\) (Section~\ref{sec:mm-layers}),
\(\vartheta=-n_0/(2(p'-p))\), and
\(c_k=(-1)^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}\binom{k-1}{\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor}\).  Then
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k=c_k(\pm\vartheta)^kd^k+O(d^{k+1})\); in particular
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) vanishes to order exactly \(k\) at \(d=0\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We read off \(\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k\) from the generic recursion by letting
\(b^2\), \(\Delta\) and \(d\) tend to \((b_\star^2,\lambda,0)\) at the same
rate.  Along such a curve only the chains of labels whose poles collide at
every step survive (Steps~1 and~2), and the limit is the lowest homogeneous
part of \(H_N\), whose behaviour at large slope \(\zeta\) gives
\([d^k](\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k)\) (Step~3).  Steps~4 and~5 list the colliding
chains and evaluate the resulting moments.

Put \(t=b^2\), \(t_\star=-p'/p\), \(N_0=2pp'\); dots denote \(d/dt\) at
\(t_\star\).  As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:d-derivatives}, write
\(\varphi(x,y)=xb+yb^{-1}\), which vanishes at \(t_\star\) iff
\((x,y)\in\mathbb Z(p,p')\), and put \(B_g=[1-u,u]\times[1-v,v]\) for a label
\(g=(u,v)\).

\emph{Step 1: the multipliers along a blow-up.}  By the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:d-derivatives}, the factors of \({\mathcal N}_g(b,d)\) vanishing
at \((t,d)=(t_\star,0)\) are \(-d\) and the factors whose restrictions to
\(d=0\) are \(\frac14\varphi(2jp,2jp')\varphi(2-2jp,2-2jp')\) with \(j\neq0\)
and \((jp,jp')\in B_g\).  At \(d=0\) their \(t\)-derivatives are
\(j\kappa_0\), where \(\kappa_0=p(p'-p)/p'\).  The pole of \({\mathcal A}_g\)
has order at most their number minus one, with equality when
\(\vartheta_g:=\partial_d{\mathcal R}_g|_{d=0}=-(p'u-pv)/(2(p'-p))\)
(Lemma~\ref{lem:d-derivatives}) is nonzero.  So along the curve
\[
 (t,\Delta,d)=(t_\star+\varepsilon,\ \lambda+\varepsilon\zeta,\ \varepsilon y)
 \qquad(\varepsilon\to0)
\]
with \(\zeta\) and \(y\) fixed,
\[
 \frac{{\mathcal R}_g}{\varepsilon}\to\vartheta_g\,y\prod_j\Bigl(1-\frac{y}{j\kappa_0}\Bigr).
\]

\emph{Step 2: the limit along the curve.}  Expand \(H_N\) by
Proposition~\ref{prop:generic-recursion} into chains \(g_1,\dots,g_n\) with
denominators \(z_{i-1}-h_{g_i}\), where \(z_0=\Delta\) and
\(z_i=h_{g_i}+\ell_{g_i}\).  For generic \(\zeta,y\), such a denominator is of
order \(\varepsilon\) if \(h_{g_i}(t_\star)=z_{i-1}(t_\star)\), and a unit
otherwise; consecutive slopes differ, as Step~4 shows.  Only the chains whose
denominators are all of order \(\varepsilon\) contribute to the limit, and \(H_N\)
tends to
\[
 T_N(\zeta,y)=\sum_{\text{colliding chains}}\ \prod_i
 \frac{\vartheta_{g_i}\,y\prod_j(1-y/(j\kappa_0))}{\zeta_{i-1}-\dot h_{g_i}},
 \qquad \zeta_0=\zeta,\quad\zeta_i=\dot h_{g_i}.
\]

\emph{Step 3: reduction to a limit in \(\zeta\).}  The denominator of
\(H_N\) is a product of powers of the factors \(\Delta-h_g(t)\).  Near
\((t_\star,\lambda)\) such a factor is a unit if
\(h_g(t_\star)\neq\lambda\), and otherwise its lowest homogeneous part in
\((t-t_\star,\Delta-\lambda)\) is \(\Delta-\lambda-\dot h_g(t-t_\star)\).
Let \(\Phi(a,e,y)\) be the lowest homogeneous part of \(H_N\) in
\((a,e,y)=(t-t_\star,\Delta-\lambda,d)\); its denominator is a nonzero
constant times a product of linear forms \(e-\dot h_ga\).  Along the curve of
Step~1, \(H_N=\varepsilon^{\deg\Phi}(\Phi(1,\zeta,y)+O(\varepsilon))\).  The
limit is \(T_N(\zeta,y)\), which is not identically zero because its
coefficient \(C_{\theta_0}\) in Step~5 is nonzero, so \(\deg\Phi=0\) and
\(T_N(\zeta,y)=\Phi(1,\zeta,y)\).  Every form \(e-\dot h_ga\) equals \(1\) at
\((a,e)=(0,1)\), so \(\Phi\) is regular there, and homogeneity of degree
\(0\) gives
\[
 [y^k]T_N(\zeta,y)=[y^k]\Phi(\zeta^{-1},1,\zeta^{-1}y)
 =\zeta^{-k}\bigl([y^k]\Phi(0,1,y)+O(\zeta^{-1})\bigr).
\]
Hence \([y^k]T_N=O(\zeta^{-k})\) and
\(\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}\zeta^k[y^k]T_N=[y^k]\Phi(0,1,y)\).  The same
expansion along \((a,e,y)=(0,\varepsilon,\varepsilon y)\) gives
\(H_N(c_\star,\lambda+\varepsilon,\varepsilon y)\to\Phi(0,1,y)\) as
\(\varepsilon\to0\).

Now take \(N=\ell^\pm_k\), so that \(j_N(\lambda)=k\), and write
\(H_N(c_\star,\lambda+x,d)=\sum_{i=1}^ka_i(d)x^{-i}+r(x,d)\) with \(r\)
regular at \(x=0\) (Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith}).  If some \(a_i\)
vanished at \(d=0\) to an order \(o_i<i\), the indices with the largest
\(i-o_i\) would give a divergent term
\(\varepsilon^{-(i-o_i)}\sum_i[d^{o_i}]a_i\,y^{o_i}\) in
\(H_N(c_\star,\lambda+\varepsilon,\varepsilon y)\), a nonzero polynomial in
\(y\) because distinct \(i\) with the same \(i-o_i\) give distinct powers of
\(y\).  So every \(a_i\) vanishes to order at least \(i\), the limit is
\(r(0,0)+\sum_i([d^i]a_i)\,y^i\), and \([y^k]\Phi(0,1,y)=[d^k]a_k\).  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, \(a_k=\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k\) when
\(\ell^\pm_k=\ell_k\), and otherwise
\(a_k=\mathcal N^\pm_k/\gamma^\pm_k
+(\mathcal N^\mp_k/\gamma^\mp_k)H_M(c_\star,\mu^\mp_k,d)\) with
\(M=\ell^\pm_k-\ell_k\geq1\).  In the second case the extra term vanishes to
order at least \(k+1\) at \(d=0\): its first factor is the \(a_k\) of the
level \(\ell_k\), of order at least \(k\), and \(H_M(c_\star,\mu^\mp_k,0)=0\)
by Lemma~\ref{lem:d-derivatives}.  Hence
\[
 [d^k]\frac{\mathcal N^\pm_k}{\gamma^\pm_k}
 =\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}\zeta^k[y^k]T_{\ell^\pm_k}.
\]

\emph{Step 4: the colliding chains.}  By integrality of \(u,v\), the colliding
chains from \(\lambda\) are the sequences \(n_0=x_0,x_1,\dots\) with
\(|x_i|\) increasing and \(x_i\) alternately in \(n_1+N_0\mathbb Z\) and
\(n_0+N_0\mathbb Z\), via
\[
 g_i=\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_i(x_{i-1}+x_i)}{2p'},\
          \frac{\epsilon_i(x_i-x_{i-1})}{2p}\Bigr),
 \qquad \epsilon_i=\operatorname{sgn}x_i.
\]
Then
\[
 \vartheta_{g_i}=-\frac{\epsilon_ix_{i-1}}{2(p'-p)},\qquad
 4p'^2\dot h_{g_i}=p'^2-p^2-x_{i-1}x_i,
\]
so \(4p'^2(\dot h_{g_{i-1}}-\dot h_{g_i})=x_{i-1}(x_i-x_{i-2})\neq0\), and
\(|x_i|\) is the momentum reached.  The endpoints \(\pm m^\pm_k\) are
\(n_1+jN_0\) and \(n_1-(j+1)N_0\) for \(k=2j+1\), and \(n_0\pm jN_0\) for
\(k=2j\).  The first steps of the chains ending there are \(x_1=n_1+iN_0\)
with \(-j\leq i\leq j\), \(-j-1\leq i\leq j-1\) and \(-j\leq i\leq j-1\) in
these three cases; these are \(k\) distinct slopes.

\emph{Step 5: the moments.}  Group the colliding chains of
\(T_{\ell^\pm_k}\) by their first step \(\theta\).  Only the first
denominator of a chain depends on \(\zeta\), so
\([y^k]T_{\ell^\pm_k}=\sum_\theta C_\theta/(\zeta-\dot h_\theta)\), summed
over the \(k\) first steps of Step~4, whose slopes \(\dot h_\theta\) are
distinct, with \(C_\theta\) independent of \(\zeta\).  Expanding in
\(\zeta^{-1}\) gives
\(\sum_{i\geq0}\zeta^{-i-1}\sum_\theta C_\theta\dot h_\theta^{\,i}\).  Since
this is \(O(\zeta^{-k})\) (Step~3), \(\sum_\theta C_\theta\dot h_\theta^{\,i}=0\)
for \(0\leq i\leq k-2\), and
\(\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}\zeta^k[y^k]T_{\ell^\pm_k}
=\sum_\theta C_\theta\dot h_\theta^{\,k-1}\).  These \(k-1\) equations form a
Vandermonde system of rank \(k-1\), so
\(C_\theta=C/\prod_{\theta'\neq\theta}(\dot h_\theta-\dot h_{\theta'})\) for a
constant \(C\).  By Lagrange interpolation,
\(\sum_\theta\dot h_\theta^{\,i}/\prod_{\theta'\neq\theta}(\dot h_\theta-\dot h_{\theta'})\)
is \(0\) for \(i\leq k-2\) and \(1\) for \(i=k-1\); so
\(\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}\zeta^k[y^k]T_{\ell^\pm_k}
=C=C_{\theta_0}\prod_{\theta\neq\theta_0}(\dot h_{\theta_0}-\dot h_\theta)\)
for each \(\theta_0\).  By Step~4 with \(x_0=n_0\),
\(4p'^2(\dot h_{\theta_0}-\dot h_\theta)=n_0(x_\theta-x_{\theta_0})\), where
\(x_\theta\) is the first momentum of \(\theta\).  Hence
\[
 \lim_{\zeta\to\infty}\zeta^k[y^k]T_{\ell^\pm_k}
 =C_{\theta_0}\prod_{\theta\neq\theta_0}\frac{n_0(x_\theta-x_{\theta_0})}{4p'^2}.
\]
The seed coefficient \(C_{\theta_0}\) below is computed from the chain
product of Step~2 alone.  For \(k=2j+1\) take \(\theta_0\) the endpoint,
\((r+jp,s+jp')\) or
\(((j+1)p-r,(j+1)p'-s)\), whose box meets \(\mathbb Z(p,p')\) for
\(|j'|\leq j\) (as \(0<r<p\), \(0<s<p'\)).  Then
\(C_{\theta_0}=\pm\vartheta(-1)^j/(j!^2\kappa_0^{2j})\), the product is
\((n_0N_0/4p'^2)^{2j}(2j)!\), and \(n_0N_0/(4p'^2\kappa_0)=-\vartheta\).  For
\(k=2j\) take \(\theta_0=n_1+(j-1)N_0\) and then the step \(g\) to the endpoint,
\((r+(2j-1)p,p'-s)\) or \((p-r,s+(2j-1)p')\), whose box meets
\(\mathbb Z(p,p')\) only at \(0\).  Then
\(\vartheta_g/(\dot h_{\theta_0}-\dot h_g)=-2p'^2/((p'-p)jN_0)\), and the product is
\(-(n_0N_0/4p'^2)^{2j-1}(2j-1)!\).  Both cases give \(c_k(\pm\vartheta)^k\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Integrality, valuation, and level polynomiality at \(d=h_{1,\pm3}(b)\)]
\label{prop:h13-nonvanishing}
Put \(t=b^2\), \(d_1=h_{1,3}(b)=-1-2/t\), \(d_2=h_{1,-3}(b)=2-2/t\),
\(P_0^{(\nu)}=1\), and for \(M\geq1\) and \(\nu\in\{1,2\}\),
\(P_M^{(\nu)}(t,Y):=M!\,t^{2M}\prod_{u=1}^M(2\Delta-2h_{u,1}(b))\,
H_M(c(b),\Delta,d_\nu)|_{\Delta=Y/t^2}\).
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}\setlength{\topsep}{3pt}
\item[(i)] For \(M\geq1\) and \(\nu\in\{1,2\}\),
\(P_M^{(\nu)}(t,Y)\in\mathbb Z[\tfrac12][t,Y]\),
\(P_M^{(2)}(0,Y)=P_M^{(1)}(0,Y)\), and \(P_M^{(\nu)}(0,0)=4^M\).
\item[(ii)] For every \(i,j\geq0\) and \(\nu\in\{1,2\}\),
\(\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M):=[t^iY^j](P_M^{(\nu)}(t,Y)/4^M)\) is a polynomial in
\(M\) of degree at most \(2i+2j\), valid for all integers \(M\geq0\).
\item[(iii)] Let \(p,p'\geq2\) be coprime, \(b_\star^2=-p'/p\), and let
\(\varpi\mid p'\) be an odd prime with \(a=v_\varpi(p')\geq1\).  If
\(M\geq1\) and \(\mu=(m^2-(p'-p)^2)/(4pp')\) (\(m\in\mathbb Z\)) satisfy
\(\det G_M(c_\star,\mu)\neq0\), then \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d_\nu)\) (\(\nu=1,2\))
is regular and nonzero with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:h13-valuation}
 v_\varpi\bigl(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d_\nu)\bigr)
 =-v_\varpi(M!)-Ma-\sum_{u=1}^M v_\varpi\bigl(m^2-(p'u-p)^2\bigr).
\end{equation}
In particular, for every coprime pair \(p,p'\geq2\) (using the symmetry
\(p\leftrightarrow p'\) when \(p'\) has no odd prime factor), every \(M\geq1\)
and every integer momentum \(m\) with
\(\det G_M(c_\star,\mu)\neq0\), one has \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d)\not\equiv0\) in
\(\mathbb Q[d]\).
\item[(iv)] In the setting of~(iii), if \(t_0\in\varpi^a\mathbb Z_\varpi\),
\(Y_1,Y_2\in\varpi^{a+1}\mathbb Z_\varpi\), and integers \(M_1,M_2\geq1\),
\(e\geq0\) satisfy \(v_\varpi(M_1-M_2)\geq e\) and \(v_\varpi(Y_1-Y_2)\geq e+a\),
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:second-screening-lip}
 v_\varpi\!\left(
 \frac{P_{M_1}^{(1)}(t_0,Y_1)\,P_{M_2}^{(2)}(t_0,Y_2)}
      {P_{M_1}^{(2)}(t_0,Y_1)\,P_{M_2}^{(1)}(t_0,Y_2)}-1\right)
 \geq e+a.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Parts (i) and (ii) are proved in Appendix~\ref{app:h13}.

(iii) Specialize to \(t_\star=-p'/p\) and \(\Delta=\mu\).  By the Kac
determinant formula, \(\det G_M(c_\star,\mu)\neq0\) gives
\(\det G_{M'}(c_\star,\mu)\neq0\) (\(M'\leq M\)) and
\(\mu\neq h_{u,1}(b_\star)\) (\(1\leq u\leq M\)), so
\(H_M(c(b),\Delta,d_\nu)\) is regular at \((b_\star,\mu)\).  Both \(t_\star\)
and \(Y_\star=\mu t_\star^2=p'(m^2-(p'-p)^2)/(4p^3)\) lie in
\(\varpi\mathbb Z_\varpi\), so~(i) gives
\(P_M^{(\nu)}(t_\star,Y_\star)\equiv4^M\not\equiv0\pmod{\varpi\mathbb Z_\varpi}\),
whence \(v_\varpi(P_M^{(\nu)}(t_\star,Y_\star))=0\) and
\(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d_\nu)\neq0\); since
\(2\mu-2h_{u,1}(b_\star)=(m^2-(p'u-p)^2)/(2pp')\), taking \(v_\varpi\) gives
\eqref{eq:h13-valuation}.  Every coprime pair \(p,p'\geq2\) has an odd prime
factor, so \(b\mapsto b^{-1}\) gives \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d)\not\equiv0\) in
general.

(iv) Fix \(\nu\in\{1,2\}\).  By~(i)
and~(ii), each \(\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M)\) has degree \(n\leq2i+2j\) and lies in
\(\mathbb Z[\tfrac12]\) for all \(M\geq0\), so
\(\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M)=\sum_{u=0}^n\beta_u\binom{M}{u}\)
(\(\beta_u\in\mathbb Z[\tfrac12]\)).  For \(n=0\),
\(\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M_1)=\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M_2)\).  For \(i+j\geq1\) and
\(1\leq u\leq n\), \((M_1-M_2)\mid u!(\binom{M_1}{u}-\binom{M_2}{u})\), so
\(v_\varpi(\binom{M_1}{u}-\binom{M_2}{u})\geq e-v_\varpi(n!)\); since \(\varpi\geq3\),
Legendre's formula gives \(v_\varpi(n!)\leq(n-1)/(\varpi-1)\leq(n-1)/2\), hence
\(v_\varpi(n!)\leq i+j-1\).  Since \(v_\varpi(t_0)\geq a\) and
\(v_\varpi(Y_1)\geq a+1\), each term with \(i+j\geq1\) in
\(P_{M_1}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_1)/4^{M_1}-P_{M_2}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_1)/4^{M_2}
=\sum_{i+j\geq1}(\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M_1)-\xi_{i,j}^{(\nu)}(M_2))t_0^iY_1^j\)
has valuation at least \(e-(i+j-1)+ia+j(a+1)=e+(i+j)(a-1)+j+1\geq e+a\).
As \(P_{M_2}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y)/4^{M_2}\in\mathbb Z_\varpi[Y]\),
\(v_\varpi(P_{M_2}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_1)/4^{M_2}-P_{M_2}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_2)/4^{M_2})
\geq v_\varpi(Y_1-Y_2)\geq e+a\).  Combining gives
\(v_\varpi(P_{M_1}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_1)/4^{M_1}-P_{M_2}^{(\nu)}(t_0,Y_2)/4^{M_2})\geq e+a\)
(\(\nu=1,2\)), and since all four values are \(\varpi\)-adic units,
\eqref{eq:second-screening-lip} follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Nonvanishing on all two-vector levels]
\label{prop:two-term-criteria}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, let
\(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<N<\ell_{k+1}\), and let \(P_N(d)\) denote the
right side of \eqref{eq:top-layer}.  Then \(P_N(d)\not\equiv0\) in
\(\mathbb Q[d]\), and \(H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)\) has a pole of order exactly
\(k\) at \(\Delta=\lambda\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Both terms of \(P_N(d)\) are present, and we show that they cannot cancel by
looking at them near \(d_1=h_{1,3}(b_\star)\) and, if needed,
\(d_2=h_{1,-3}(b_\star)\), where Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}
controls the shifted blocks.  In Case~1 the two terms vanish to different
orders at \(d_1\).  In Case~2 they vanish to the same order, and their leading
coefficients at \(d_1\) have different \(\varpi\)-adic valuations.  In Case~3 the
valuations can agree, and we show instead that the ratio of the two leading
coefficients at \(d_1\) differs from the ratio at \(d_2\).

By \(b\mapsto b^{-1}\) (swapping \(p\leftrightarrow p'\),
\(r\leftrightarrow s\)), assume an odd prime \(\varpi\mid p'\) with
\(a=v_\varpi(p')\geq1\).  Write \(M^\pm=N-\ell^\pm_k\geq1\) and, with
\(d_x\) as in Section~\ref{sec:mm-layers},
\[
 x_0=|p'-p|,\qquad x_1=|p'-3p|,\qquad x_2=p'+3p,
\]
so that \(d_{x_0}=0\), \(d_{x_1}=d_1=h_{1,3}(b_\star)\) and
\(d_{x_2}=d_2=h_{1,-3}(b_\star)\).  With \(w^\pm_k\) normalized as after
Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, write
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k(d)=\prod_x(d-d_x)^{o^\pm_k(x)}\), so that
\(o^\pm_k(x)=\ord_{d=d_x}\mathcal N^\pm_k\); the factorization of
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) into the generic norms \({\mathcal N}_{R,S}\) is the one
displayed there.  Moreover \(M^+-M^-=\ell^-_k-\ell^+_k\) equals \(k(pp'-n_1)>0\) for odd \(k\)
and \(-kn_0\neq0\) for even \(k\).  Put \(e_\pm:=o^\pm_k(x_1)\), the
multiplicity of \(d_1\) in \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\).

\emph{Case 1: \(s\in\{1,p'-1\}\).}  Solve \(|p'a'\pm pl|=|p'-3p|\) in odd
positive integers \((a',l)\).  The plus sign gives \(p'(a'+1)=p(3-l)\), which
forces \(l=1\) and \(p'\mid2\), impossible as \(\varpi\mid p'\), or
\(p'(a'-1)=-p(l+3)\), which is impossible.  The minus sign gives
\(p'(a'-1)=p(l-3)\) or \(p'(a'+1)=p(l+3)\), with solutions
\((1+2ip,3+2ip')\) (\(i\geq0\)) and \((2ip-1,2ip'-3)\) (\(i\geq1\)).  Thus the
multiplicity of \(d_1\) in \({\mathcal N}_{R,S}\) is
\[
 \#\{i\geq0:ip\leq R-1,\ ip'\leq S-2\}+\#\{i\geq1:ip\leq R,\ ip'\leq S+1\}.
\]
Evaluating on \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) gives
\[
 \begin{array}{c|cc}
  & e_+ & e_-\\\hline
  k=2j+1 & 2j+\mathbf 1_{s\geq2} & 2j+\mathbf 1_{s\leq p'-2}\\
  k=2j & 2j-1+\mathbf 1_{s\leq p'-2} & 2j-\mathbf 1_{s=1}
 \end{array}
\]
Hence \(e_+\neq e_-\) if and only if \(s\in\{1,p'-1\}\), and \(e_+=e_-=k\)
otherwise.  When \(e_+\neq e_-\), extracting
\([(d-d_1)^{\min(e_+,e_-)}]P_N(d)\) removes the branch of higher order and
leaves a nonzero multiple of \(H_{M^{\min}}(c_\star,\mu^{\min}_k,d_1)\), which
is nonzero by Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iii).

\emph{Case 2: \(2\leq s\leq p'-2\) and \(s\not\equiv\pm1\pmod\varpi\).}  Now
\(e_+=e_-=k\).  By Proposition~\ref{prop:layer-coefficients},
\(\gamma^\pm_k=[d^k]\mathcal N^\pm_k/(c_k(\pm\vartheta)^k)\).  We have
\begin{align*}
 [(d-d_1)^k]\mathcal N^\pm_k&=(4pp')^{k-2\ell^\pm_k}\prod_{x\neq x_1}(x_1^2-x^2)^{o^\pm_k(x)},\\
 [d^k]\mathcal N^\pm_k&=(4pp')^{k-2\ell^\pm_k}\prod_{x\neq x_0}(x_0^2-x^2)^{o^\pm_k(x)}.
\end{align*}
Combined with~\eqref{eq:h13-valuation}, this shows that the two summands of
\([(d-d_1)^k]P_N(d)\) have \(\varpi\)-adic valuations differing by
\(V_\varpi^+(N)-V_\varpi^-(N)\), where
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 V_\varpi^\pm(N):={}&\sum_{x\neq x_1}o^\pm_k(x)\,v_\varpi(x_1^2-x^2)
 -\sum_{x\neq x_0}o^\pm_k(x)\,v_\varpi(x_0^2-x^2)\\
 &-v_\varpi(M^\pm!)-M^\pm a-\sum_{u=1}^{M^\pm}v_\varpi\bigl((m^\pm_k)^2-(p'u-p)^2\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
\]
We show that the first two sums give the same total for both signs.  In
every factor \({\mathcal N}_{R,S}\) of \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\), one has
\(S\equiv\pm s\not\equiv\pm1\pmod\varpi\).

If \(\varpi\geq5\), the odd indices \(1\leq l<2S\) with
\(l\equiv\pm3\pmod\varpi\) pair bijectively with those with
\(l\equiv\pm1\pmod\varpi\) via \((2i\varpi+3\epsilon,\,2i\varpi+\epsilon)\), with
\(\epsilon=+1\), \(i\geq0\) and with \(\epsilon=-1\), \(i\geq1\).  For
\(\beta\in\{1,3\}\), \(\sigma\in\{\pm1\}\) and odd \(a'\), put
\(A=p'-\beta p\) and \(B=p'a'+\sigma p(2i\varpi+\beta\epsilon)\).  Then
\[
 A^2-B^2=(A+\epsilon\sigma B)(A-\epsilon\sigma B),\qquad
 A+\epsilon\sigma B=p'(1+\epsilon\sigma a')+2\epsilon i\varpi p,
\]
where \(A+\epsilon\sigma B\) does not depend on \(\beta\), and
\(A-\epsilon\sigma B\equiv-2\beta p\not\equiv0\pmod\varpi\) (as \(\varpi\mid p'\)).
So the paired factors either both vanish or have the same \(\varpi\)-adic
valuation.

If \(\varpi=3\), then \(3\mid s\) and \(3\mid S\), so the odd indices
\(1\leq l<2S\) partition into triples \(\{6i+1,6i+3,6i+5\}\)
(\(0\leq i<S/3\)).  Fix an odd \(a'\) and a triple.  At \(l=6i+3\) one has
\(v_3(x_0^2-x^2)=0\), while \((x_1^2-(p'a'+pl)^2)(x_1^2-(p'a'-pl)^2)\) has the
\(3\)-divisible factors
\[
 p'(1+a')+6ip,\quad p'(1-a')-6(i+1)p,\quad p'(1+a')-6(i+1)p,\quad p'(1-a')+6ip.
\]
At \(l=6i+1\) and \(l=6i+5\) one has \(v_3(x_1^2-x^2)=0\), and the
\(3\)-divisible factors of \((x_0^2-(p'a'+pl)^2)(x_0^2-(p'a'-pl)^2)\) are the
first and fourth of these (at \(6i+1\)) and the second and third (at
\(6i+5\)).  Whenever one of them vanishes (at \(x=x_1\)), the \(x_1\)-sum skips
both it and its partner \(2(p'-3p)\), whereas the \(x_0\)-sum skips the
matching zero, whose partner \(\pm2(p'-p)\) is a \(3\)-adic unit, and keeps
\(2(p'-3p)\).  Thus the first two sums in \(V_3^\pm(N)\) total
\(-e_\pm v_3(p'-3p)=-kv_3(p'-3p)\), independent of the sign.

In both cases these sums cancel in \(V_\varpi^+(N)-V_\varpi^-(N)\).  Since
\(m^+_k\equiv(-1)^{k+1}ps\) and \(m^-_k\equiv-(-1)^{k+1}ps\pmod{p'}\) give
\((m^\pm_k)^2-(p'u-p)^2\equiv p^2(s^2-1)\not\equiv0\pmod\varpi\), the last sum
also vanishes, leaving
\[
 V_\varpi^+(N)-V_\varpi^-(N)=-\bigl(v_\varpi(M^+!)-v_\varpi(M^-!)\bigr)-(M^+-M^-)a,
\]
which is nonzero because \(M^+\neq M^-\), \(a\geq1\) and \(v_\varpi(M!)\) does not
decrease with \(M\).  Two numbers of different \(\varpi\)-adic valuation do not
cancel, so \([(d-d_1)^k]P_N(d)\neq0\).

\emph{Case 3: \(2\leq s\leq p'-2\) and \(s\equiv\pm1\pmod\varpi\).}  Solving
\(|p'a'\pm pl|=p'+3p\) in odd positive \((a',l)\) yields \((1,3)\),
\((1+2ip,2ip'-3)\) and \((2ip-1,2ip'+3)\) (\(i\geq1\)); counting on
\(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) as in Case~1 gives
\(\ord_{d=d_2}\mathcal N^+_k=\ord_{d=d_2}\mathcal N^-_k=k\).  Put
\(x_1(\varepsilon)=p'-3p+\varepsilon\) and
\(x_2(\varepsilon)=p'+3p-\varepsilon\).  As \(a'\) runs over odd
\(1\leq a'<2R\), \(A\in\{1-a',1+a'\}\) runs once over
\(\{0,\pm2,\ldots,\pm2(R-1),2R\}\), so
\[
 \frac{{\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d_{x_1(\varepsilon)})}
      {{\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d_{x_2(\varepsilon)})}
 =\prod_{A}\prod_{\text{odd }1\leq l<2S}
 \frac{(p'A-p(3+l)+\varepsilon)(p'A-p(3-l)+\varepsilon)}
      {(p'A+p(3+l)-\varepsilon)(p'A+p(3-l)-\varepsilon)}.
\]
Sending \(A\mapsto-A\) in the denominator cancels every
\(A\in\{0,\pm2,\ldots,\pm2(R-1)\}\) (with \((-1)^{2S}=+1\)), leaving
\(A=2R\).  For each factor \({\mathcal N}_{R,S}\) of \(\mathcal N^\pm_k\) that is not
common to both signs, \(p\nmid R\), so no factor \(2Rp'\pm2ip\) vanishes at
\(\varepsilon=0\).  The values \(3\pm l\) form \(\{4-2S,6-2S,\dots,2S+2\}\);
cancelling \(\{4-2S,\dots,2S-4\}\) with its negation leaves
\(\{2S-2,2S,2S+2\}\), so
\[
 \lim_{\varepsilon\to0}
 \frac{{\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d_{x_1(\varepsilon)})}
      {{\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d_{x_2(\varepsilon)})}
 =\prod_{\epsilon\in\{-1,0,1\}}\frac{Rp'-Sp-\epsilon p}{Rp'+Sp+\epsilon p}.
\]
Since
\[
 d_{x_1(\varepsilon)}-d_1=\tfrac{p'-3p}{2pp'}\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^2),\qquad
 d_{x_2(\varepsilon)}-d_2=-\tfrac{p'+3p}{2pp'}\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^2)
\]
(with \(p'\neq3p\) as \(\gcd(p,p')=1\) and \(p\geq2\)), and
\(\ord_{d_1}\mathcal N^\pm_k=\ord_{d_2}\mathcal N^\pm_k=k\), the prefactors
\((-(p'-3p)/(p'+3p))^k\) cancel in the ratio of the two branches.  For both
parities of \(k\), the factors \((R^\pm,S^\pm)\) not common to both signs
satisfy \(R^+p'-S^+p=-(R^-p'-S^-p)\notin\{0,\pm p\}\) (since \(p\nmid r\),
hence \(p\nmid(R^+p'-S^+p)\)) and \(R^\pm p'+S^\pm p=m^\pm_k\).  With
\(y=R^+p'-S^+p\), the limits above for these factors have the numerators and
denominators
\[
 \begin{aligned}
  \prod_{\epsilon\in\{-1,0,1\}}(R^-p'-S^-p-\epsilon p)
  &=\prod_{\epsilon}(-y-\epsilon p)=-\prod_{\epsilon}(y-\epsilon p),\\
  \prod_{\epsilon\in\{-1,0,1\}}(R^\pm p'+S^\pm p+\epsilon p)
  &=m^\pm_k\bigl((m^\pm_k)^2-p^2\bigr),
 \end{aligned}
\]
so the numerators of the two signs have ratio \(-1\), and
\[
 X_k:=\frac{[(d-d_1)^k]\mathcal N^+_k\big/[(d-d_1)^k]\mathcal N^-_k}
          {[(d-d_2)^k]\mathcal N^+_k\big/[(d-d_2)^k]\mathcal N^-_k}
 =-\frac{m^-_k\bigl((m^-_k)^2-p^2\bigr)}{m^+_k\bigl((m^+_k)^2-p^2\bigr)}.
\]
Using \(m^+_k+m^-_k=2kpp'\) and factoring \((m^+_k)^3+(m^-_k)^3\) gives
\[
 X_k-1=\frac{-2kpp'\bigl(3(m^+_k)^2-p^2-6kpp'm^+_k+4k^2p^2p'^2\bigr)}
            {m^+_k\bigl((m^+_k)^2-p^2\bigr)}.
\]
Because \(m^+_k\equiv\pm ps\equiv\pm p\pmod\varpi\) and \(\varpi\nmid2p\), both
\(m^+_k\) and the parenthesized factor (\(\equiv2p^2\pmod\varpi\)) are
\(\varpi\)-adic units, while \(v_\varpi((m^+_k)^2-p^2)\geq1\).  Hence either
\(v_\varpi(X_k)\neq0\) or
\[
 v_\varpi(X_k-1)=v_\varpi(k)+a-v_\varpi\bigl((m^+_k)^2-p^2\bigr)\leq v_\varpi(k)+a-1.
\]
Put \(t_0=-p'/p\) and \(Y^\pm=p'((m^\pm_k)^2-(p'-p)^2)/(4p^3)\).  Then
\(v_\varpi(t_0)=a\), \(v_\varpi(Y^\pm)\geq a+1\) (as
\((m^\pm_k)^2\equiv p^2\pmod\varpi\)), \(v_\varpi(M^+-M^-)\geq v_\varpi(k)\), and
\(Y^+-Y^-=p'(2kpp')(m^+_k-m^-_k)/(4p^3)\) has
\(v_\varpi(Y^+-Y^-)\geq v_\varpi(k)+2a\).  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iii),
\(H_{M^\pm}(c_\star,\mu^\pm_k,d_\nu)\) is regular and nonzero for \(\nu=1,2\).
Put
\[
 H_{\mathrm{rat}}:=\frac{H_{M^+}(d_1)/H_{M^+}(d_2)}{H_{M^-}(d_1)/H_{M^-}(d_2)};
\]
the common denominators \(\prod_u(2\mu^\pm_k-2h_{u,1})\) cancel in this ratio.
Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iii) gives
\(v_\varpi(H_{\mathrm{rat}})=0\), and
Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing}(iv) with \(e=v_\varpi(k)\) gives
\(v_\varpi(H_{\mathrm{rat}}-1)\geq v_\varpi(k)+a\).  If \(v_\varpi(X_k)\neq0\), then
\(v_\varpi(X_kH_{\mathrm{rat}})\neq0\); otherwise
\(X_kH_{\mathrm{rat}}-1=(X_k-1)H_{\mathrm{rat}}+(H_{\mathrm{rat}}-1)\) has
valuation \(v_\varpi(X_k-1)\leq v_\varpi(k)+a-1\).  So \(X_kH_{\mathrm{rat}}\neq1\).
If \([(d-d_1)^k]P_N(d)\) and \([(d-d_2)^k]P_N(d)\) both vanished, then at
\(d_\nu\) (\(\nu=1,2\)) the leading coefficients
\([(d-d_\nu)^k]\mathcal N^\pm_k\,H_{M^\pm}(d_\nu)/\gamma^\pm_k\) of the two
summands of \(P_N\) would be opposite, and dividing their ratio at \(d_1\) by
their ratio at \(d_2\) removes \(\gamma^\pm_k\) and gives
\(X_kH_{\mathrm{rat}}=1\).  Hence \(P_N\not\equiv0\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Sharp pole order for \(H_N\)]
\label{cor:top-layer-sharp}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer}, for every coprime pair
\(p,p'\geq2\), every interior Kac weight \(\lambda=h_{r,s}(b_\star)\), and
every level \(N\geq1\), the coefficient \(H_N(c_\star,\Delta,d)\), viewed as
a rational function of \(\Delta\) over \(\mathbb Q(b_\star,d)\) with \(d\)
indeterminate, has pole order exactly \(j_N(\lambda)\) at \(\Delta=\lambda\),
where order zero means regularity.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(k=j_N(\lambda)\).  If \(k=0\), Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith} gives
regularity.  Suppose \(k\geq1\), so \(\ell_k\leq N<\ell_{k+1}\).  On a single-vector level
\(\ell_k\leq N<\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)\), only the sign with
\(\ell^\pm_k=\ell_k\) contributes to \eqref{eq:top-layer}, giving
\((\mathcal N/\gamma)H_M(c_\star,\mu,d)\) with \(M=N-\ell_k\),
\(\mu=\lambda+\ell_k\), and \(\mathcal N\neq0\).  For \(M=0\) one has \(H_0=1\);
for \(M\geq1\), Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer} gives
\(\det G_M(c_\star,\mu)\neq0\) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:h13-nonvanishing} gives \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,d)\not\equiv0\).
At \(N=\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)\), \eqref{eq:top-layer} reads
\((\mathcal N/\gamma)H_M(c_\star,\mu,d)+\mathcal N'/\gamma'\) with \(M\geq1\); by
Lemma~\ref{lem:d-derivatives}, \(H_M(c_\star,\mu,0)=0\), so by
Proposition~\ref{prop:layer-coefficients} the first term vanishes at \(d=0\) to
order at least \(k+1\) and the second to order exactly \(k\), whence the sum is
nonzero.  Every two-vector level
\(\max(\ell^+_k,\ell^-_k)<N<\ell_{k+1}\) is settled by
Proposition~\ref{prop:two-term-criteria}.
\end{proof}

\section{The Ising model}\label{sec:ising}

At \(c=1/2\) one has \(b_\star^2=-4/3\), \((p,p')=(3,4)\), and
\(h_{r,s}(b_\star)=((4r-3s)^2-1)/48\).  The interior weights are the energy
weight \(\tfrac12\), the spin weight \(\tfrac1{16}\) and the identity weight
\(0\), with labels \((r,s)=(2,1),(1,2),(1,1)\) and \(n_0=5,-2,1\).  Their
layer levels (Section~\ref{sec:mm-layers}) and the labels with \(rs\leq6\) that
give the same weight are
\[
\begin{array}{ccccc}
\lambda&\text{labels with }rs\leq6&(\ell^+_1,\ell^-_1)&
 (\ell^+_2,\ell^-_2)&(\ell^+_3,\ell^-_3)\\ \hline
1/2&(2,1),(1,3)&(2,3)&(17,7)&(25,28)\\
1/16&(1,2),(2,2)&(2,4)&(10,14)&(24,30)\\
0&(1,1),(2,3)&(1,6)&(13,11)&(20,35)
\end{array}
\]

\begin{proposition}[Ising double poles]\label{prop:ising-double-poles}
For \(N<7\) every pole of \(H_N(\tfrac12,\Delta,d)\) in \(\Delta\) is
simple.  Over \(\mathbb Q(d)\), the first double pole at each interior weight
\(\lambda\) occurs at the level \(N\) shown in the table, and its leading
Laurent coefficient is
\(\lim_{\Delta\to\lambda}(\Delta-\lambda)^2H_N(\tfrac12,\Delta,d)=\mathcal N(d)/\gamma\):
\[
\begin{array}{cclr}
\lambda&N&\mathcal N(d)&\gamma\\ \hline
1/2&7&{\mathcal N}_{2,1}{\mathcal N}_{1,5}&-700700\\
1/16&10&{\mathcal N}_{1,2}{\mathcal N}_{4,2}&-1121229484375/192\\
0&11&{\mathcal N}_{1,1}{\mathcal N}_{2,5}&-2477917160468750/6561
\end{array}
\]
Here \({\mathcal N}_{R,S}={\mathcal N}_{R,S}(b_\star,d)\) is the polynomial of
Definition~\ref{def:generic-residue}, monic of degree \(2RS\) in \(d\), and
\(\mathcal N\) and \(\gamma\) are the torus and second Jantzen norms of the singular
vector that starts the second Jantzen layer.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(rs\leq6\), the only repeated values of \(|4r-3s|\) are \(1,2,5\), giving
the three classes in the table. Consider a
weight with a single label \(a\) of level \(\ell_a\leq N\).  The injective
singular embedding \cite[the paragraph preceding Corollary~5.2]{IoharaKoga2011}
puts \(p(N-\ell_a)\) independent vectors in the radical.  This number is the
valuation of the Kac determinant, so all positive Smith exponents are \(1\)
(Lemma~\ref{lem:smith}).  At the three interior weights,
Theorem~\ref{thm:jantzen-smith} gives \(j_N(\lambda)\leq1\) below the
second-layer levels \(7\), \(10\) and \(11\).  At those levels~\eqref{eq:top-layer} has the single term \(\mathcal N/\gamma\),
with \(M=0\). The factors of \(\mathcal N\) are given by the factorization
after Theorem~\ref{thm:top-layer} with \(j=1\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:layer-coefficients} with \(c_2=-1\) gives
\(\gamma=-[d^2]\mathcal N/\vartheta^2\), where \(\vartheta=-n_0/2\).
\end{proof}

The ancillary scripts compare the norms \(\gamma\) at \(N=7\) and \(N=10\)
with direct PBW evaluations of Lemma~\ref{lem:top-coefficient}(iii), and
reconstruct the double-pole coefficient by Shapovalov inversion at \(N=7\)
and, in a longer run, at \(N=10\) (see the code availability section).
\begin{center}\large\bfseries Part II.\enspace Sphere four-point blocks\end{center}

\section{The sphere four-point block}\label{sec:setup-sph}

Part~II uses the Verma module \(M(c,\Delta)\), its PBW basis and the
Shapovalov Gram matrices \(G_N(c,\Delta)\) of Section~\ref{sec:blocks}, and
writes \(v_\Delta=\lvert\Delta\rangle\) for the highest-weight vector and
\(\langle u,v\rangle\) for the Shapovalov form.  In Part~II, \(H_N\) denotes
Zamolodchikov's elliptic coefficient of the sphere block
(Definition~\ref{def:H-series}), not the eta-reduced torus coefficient of
Part~I.

\begin{definition}[Three-point pairing and sphere block]\label{def:sphere-block}
For indeterminate weights \(\Delta,\Delta_a,\Delta_b\) and a partition
\(Y=(y_1\geq\dots\geq y_r\geq1)\), define the three-point polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gamma-Y}
 \Gamma_Y(\Delta;\Delta_a,\Delta_b)
 :=\prod_{j=1}^r\Bigl(\Delta+\sum_{i=j+1}^r y_i+y_j\Delta_b-\Delta_a\Bigr)
 \in\mathbb Z[\Delta,\Delta_a,\Delta_b],
\end{equation}
with \(\Gamma_\emptyset=1\).  Let \(\langle u\mid V_{\Delta_b}(1)\mid\Delta_a\rangle\)
denote the unique linear functional on \(M(c,\Delta)\) with
\(\langle v_\Delta\mid V_{\Delta_b}(1)\mid\Delta_a\rangle=1\) and
\(\langle L_{-n}u\mid V_{\Delta_b}(1)\mid\Delta_a\rangle
=(h(u)+n\Delta_b-\Delta_a)\langle u\mid V_{\Delta_b}(1)\mid\Delta_a\rangle\) for
homogeneous \(u\) of \(L_0\)-weight \(h(u)\) and \(n\geq1\).  Induction on
\(r\) gives \(\langle L_{-Y}v_\Delta\mid V_{\Delta_b}(1)\mid\Delta_a\rangle
=\Gamma_Y(\Delta;\Delta_a,\Delta_b)\).

The \(s\)-channel Verma sphere four-point block is
\[
 \mathcal F(c,\Delta,\Delta_i;z)
 =z^{\Delta-\Delta_1-\Delta_2}\mathcal B(c,\Delta,\Delta_i;z),
 \qquad
 \mathcal B(c,\Delta,\Delta_i;z)
 =\sum_{N=0}^\infty\mathcal B_N(c,\Delta,\Delta_i)\,z^N,
\]
with \(\mathcal B_0=1\) and, for \(N\geq1\),
\begin{multline}\label{eq:B-def}
 \mathcal B_N(c,\Delta,\Delta_i)
 :=\sum_{|Y|=|Z|=N}\Gamma_Y(\Delta;\Delta_4,\Delta_3)\,
   \bigl(G_N(c,\Delta)^{-1}\bigr)_{Y,Z}\,\Gamma_Z(\Delta;\Delta_1,\Delta_2)\\
 \in\mathbb Q(c,\Delta)[\Delta_1,\Delta_2,\Delta_3,\Delta_4].
\end{multline}
\end{definition}
